# Backlog — Open-Loops Ledger

> OPEN LOOPS is reconciled at every session open and close. Nothing is tracked only in chat.
> An item leaves OPEN LOOPS only by meeting its close-criterion or being explicitly tombstoned.
> When writing a close criterion, state what its instrument reads if the work never happened. If that's the same as success, it isn't an instrument. See oddities, "a close criterion whose instrument can't fail." A WATCH states the calendar date its read is owed, not only the event: HO 756's committees WATCH read *after the 28th scheduled tick* and went unread for a month past Oct 6, because a reconcile counts lines and doesn't read dates (HO 804).
> A backlog line's stated **mechanism/premise** is a claim, not a fact — probe it before building on it, exactly like a logged bug's repro. Two lines in three HOs were wrong on inspection (HO 512's "`getNewsFeed` already joins `observation_entities`"; HO 514's "the spine is SOURCES"), both caught by checking, not trusting. A third would make it a convention problem, not coincidence. **The third arrived at HO 741, and it names the convention: a premise measured at a DERIVED STORE is a measurement of the pipeline that fills it, not of the world.** The HO 736 line said "measured at the seeds rather than inferred — no rater rates Alaska's at-large seat"; all three rate it Likely R. The measurement was real and it measured the ingest's blind spot: `race-ratings-scrape.ts:112-119` drops every at-large row by construction, on a premise HO 711 retired. **So a line resting on a derived store says which store, and what the pipeline that fills it cannot see.**
> **The reconcile instrument, and its control (HO 740).** *live* = `grep -cE '^- \*\*'` · *struck* = `grep -cE '^- ~~'`; the two must sum to `grep -cE '^- '`, and **`grep -cE '^- \*\*~~'` must read 0** — a nonzero there is a MIS-STRUCK line, not a count. Five lines wore `- **~~` (a strike wrapped in bold), so every reconcile from HO 660 to HO 739 read five too high on both sides; they were normalised at HO 740 and the pair moved 250/285 → 245/290 on an unchanged total of 535.
> **Since HO 814 the struck lines are in `docs/backlog-archive.md`,** moved verbatim under the section each was struck in; nothing is deleted. Strike a line here with its close note, then run `node scripts/diagnostic/docs-diet-814.mjs --write` in the same commit, and DONE's tombstones go the same way. The instrument reads both files: *live* = `grep -cE '^- \*\*' docs/backlog.md` · *struck* = `cat docs/backlog.md docs/backlog-archive.md | grep -cE '^- ~~'` · the two sum to `cat docs/backlog.md docs/backlog-archive.md | grep -cE '^- '`, and `grep -cE '^- \*\*~~'` reads 0 over both. `grep -cE '^- \*\*' docs/backlog-archive.md` reads 0: a live line in the archive is a line lost. At the move the pair read 269 / 392 on 661, as before it.
> Sections: OPEN LOOPS (dated, owned) · QUEUED (next builds) · BANKED (gated) · WATCH (standing observations) · FIT & FINISH (audit findings) · DONE (tombstones, append-only).

Owners: **Corey** (human action) · **Code** (a build/run) · **cron** (happens on schedule).

---

- **The feed's open-on-NEW rule reads NEW's cached count, and NEW's empty state reads its separately cached rows, so for minutes after the 7-day window rolls the two can disagree (HO 797's review).** opened 2026-11-01; *Owner:* Code, low. `app/page.tsx` opens the feed on MOVERS when `newBillsCount === 0`, and `newBillsCount` is `getNewBillsThisWeekCount` (cached, tag `bills`, `lib/queries.ts:695`); `NewThisWeek` renders *No new bills introduced in the last 7 days.* off `getNewBillsThisWeek`'s rows (tags `bills` and `news-breaking`, `:772`). - **A same-day second update that Congress.gov indexes late can still be missed (HO 790).** opened 2026-10-24; *Owner:* Code, low. Since HO 790 the frontier listing fetches every entry the list's filter returns, so a second update on the day CT stored the first is read when Congress.gov indexes it in time (`scripts/diagnostic/sync-frontier-790.ts`, leg F). The frontier moves to a completed listing's start less 15 minutes, so an update indexed more than 15 minutes after its `updateDateIncludingText` falls below it, and the one listing left to reach it is the daily wide listing, whose compare is at day precision: a bill stored earlier the same day reads current and isn't queued (`lib/sync.ts`, `walkList`'s day compare; leg H's h2 is that shape). How often Congress.gov indexes that late is unmeasured. *Instrument:* not built. The HO 790 handoff named `bills-gap-788.ts --census`, run monthly, but the census classes at day precision too (its compare is the old `stored >= listUpdate`), so it reads this shape current whether or not it happens; the reading that can see it is the detail's instant against the stored one, for a sample of bills stored on their own list day, one detail read each. *Close:* that sample reading 0 behind on three monthly runs, or a list field that carries a time. *Reads if never done:* the census reads 0 behind, which is what it reads either way. **HO 791 (2026-10-26): the instrument is built; run 1 of 3 reads 47 same-day misses, none of them the margin's.** `scripts/diagnostic/late-index-791.ts --sample` draws a seeded sample of the bills whose `update_date` falls in the last `--days` and makes one detail read each. It sets the detail's instant (U2) against the stored one (U1) and the frontier (F), and classes each bill current, pending, same-day miss, wide-reachable, wide-unreachable or ahead. Before any class prints, a control moves ten sampled U1s in memory and must read five same-day misses and five wide-unreachable. Run 1 (seed 1793058413, 5:49 PM MT Oct 26, 23:49Z; 500 of 670) read 436 current, 17 pending, **47 same-day misses** and 0 of the rest (`docs/probes/791-late-index.md`). Every miss has U2 before 13:53:37Z Oct 24, the frontier's bootstrap instant, so the old code left each one behind. 24 of them were inside the first tick's wide window, and its day compare passed them over: this line's shape, running live. The 16 sampled bills stored since the frontier began all read current, too few to measure the margin, so the margin question is open. The 47 are filed as their own line, *Bills the old sync left behind by a same-day second update sit below every listing*, and until that line closes a run's misses from before the frontier are its reading: this line reads the rows with U1 at or after 13:53:37Z Oct 24. The first run's control failed on a defect in the handoff's classes, which overlapped where U2 is beyond the wide window on U1's day; same-day now comes first. The close is unchanged: three monthly runs reading 0, or a list field that carries a time. **HO 793 (2026-10-27): the shape now needs a lag over 48 hours.** Since HO 793 the daily wide listing queues every entry in its 48-hour window, whatever the stored day says (`lib/sync.ts`, the day compare and `getStoredUpdates` removed), so a same-day second update Congress.gov indexes late is fetched at the next 00:00Z tick if the lag is under 48 hours. Leg L of `scripts/diagnostic/sync-frontier-790.ts` reads it: red at `67e9575`, green at `5b6dbe3`. HO 792's twelve (`119-hr-10885` to `119-hr-10906`, at least 37 minutes late on a later day, taken by tick #28520) are the first reading of late indexing past the 15-minute margin. The close stays: the sampler's monthly runs reading 0, now over the residual shape, a lag over 48 hours.

- **A law can read short of `enacted`: `computeStage` reads the latest action alone, and paperwork filed after a bill became law can be that action (HO 812).** opened 2026-11-10; *Owner:* Code, medium; **dated: after GradShow, Thursday 2026-11-19** (`date -u -d 2026-11-19 +%A`). `119-hr-1043` became P.L. 119-68 on 2025-12-29. Both are before the deadline only if every tick advances and each visit writes: a House visit that times out or finds no section, or a Senate visit that is not `ok`, moves the cursor on without writing (read in code, `:1267-1295` and `:910-922`). **The read owed on 2026-10-12:** DE-AL's and S-DE's `primary_candidates` statuses (`primary_id IN ('house-DE-00-2026-R', 'senate-DE-2026-R')`). If either still has no `winner`, run a targeted re-read of Delaware before 2026-10-15 12:00Z: `npm run reingest:primary-slate -- DE 2026-09-15`. It writes prod, but not through the normal sync path. It fetches the live pages (the House read passes `bypassCache: true`, `scripts/reingest-primary-slate.ts:92-93`) and UPDATEs `vote_pct` and `status` keyed on `(primary_id, name)` (`:107-110`), with no `isSettled` check, so it also works after the deadline. - **A Senate roll that keeps failing is now fetched on every run, and one stuck at 502/503 costs four GETs and ~9s each time, attempted before the day's new rolls (HO 746).** opened 2026-09-24; *Owner:* Code, low. **Read in code at `4b89024`, not measured, filed from HO 746.** Since the stored-set skip (`lib/senate-votes-sync.ts`, `getStoredRolls` and the membership skip), any menu roll with no row is fetched on every run. What that costs depends on how it fails. `fetchXml` throws on the first response for a 404 or 500, which is one GET a run. It retries a 502/503 or a network error three times, with 1.5 + 3 + 4.5 s of backoff: four GETs and about 9s a run. The loop ascends (`[...menuVotes].reverse()`), so gap rolls below the stored max are attempted before the day's new rolls. All of it happens inside the route's 55s soft budget, which the House leg shares and uses first (`app/api/sync-votes/route.ts` runs `runVotesSync` before `runSenateVotesSync`). So a few rolls stuck at 502/503 would eat the Senate leg's share and push new votes behind them. A tick that times out stores no sync stats, so `votesFailed` and `votesFilled` would vanish on exactly those ticks. **Never observed:** `votesFailed` was 0 in all 117 ticks that carry Senate stats (the HO 745 probe's history, read 2026-09-24 after #19740). **Candidate fixes, not built:** a per-run cap on gap rolls, like `HEAL_CAP`, or attempting rolls above the stored max first. *Close:* a leg on a `file:` copy, with several rolls below the max forced to 503 and one new roll above it, in which the new roll is written in the same run and the run's elapsed stays bounded. *Reads if never done:* the new roll waits behind the stuck ones, and elapsed grows by ~9s per stuck roll.
- **Roll calls recorded after the last vote-sync tick before 3 January are never fetched, in either chamber, because `getCurrentCongress` switches over (HO 746).** opened 2026-09-24; *Owner:* Code, low. **Read in code at `4b89024`, not measured, filed from HO 746. It predates HO 746.** Both syncs reach only `getCurrentCongress()`'s sessions `[1, 2]` (`opts.congress ?? getCurrentCongress()` in `lib/senate-votes-sync.ts` and `lib/votes-sync.ts`). `lib/congress.ts` moves to the next congress at 00:00Z on 3 January. So the last `0 10 * * *` tick that still syncs the 119th is 2027-01-02 10:00Z, and any roll either chamber records in 119/2 after it is never fetched by the cron. The stored-set skip doesn't reach it, because that session is no longer read. **The measurement that would test it:** after 2027-01-03, compare the 119/2 Senate menu and the House's 119/2 list against the store for rolls above the stored max. That needs a congress override; the 745 probe reads only the current congress. **Candidate fix, not built:** keep syncing the previous congress's last session for a few days after the switch. *Close:* a run after the switch, or a leg with its clock set past it on a copy missing the 119/2 tail, fetches the rolls recorded after the last 119th tick. *Reads if never done:* on 2027-01-04, 119/2 rolls above the stored max that no scheduled run will fetch.
- **`/api/health` counts `timeout` as alive and fresh on every route, which is right for a cron that resumes next tick and wrong for an all-or-nothing one (HO 744).** opened 2026-09-23; *Owner:* Code, **outside HO 744's scope** (filed from it), low: no watched route has recorded `timeout` in the last 30 days, and the most recent anywhere is `/api/sync` #9165 on 2026-08-12. **Mechanism, read at `af42691`:** `wrapCronRoute` races each handler against a 55s soft timeout, records `timeout`, and does not cancel the handler (`lib/cron-log.ts`, `DEFAULT_SOFT_TIMEOUT_MS` and the `CronTimeoutError` branch). `lib/cron-health.ts` puts `timeout` in `ALIVE` beside `success` and counts it toward freshness (`status IN ('success','timeout')`). Its header gives the reason — "time-budgeted routes hit the 55s soft cap AFTER the primary work commits" — which is true of one class of route, not all of them. **Measured, HO 744:** the ratings route's control leg (per-fetch cap removed, one widget hung) recorded `timeout` at 55,018 ms, read `healthy: true`, and neither chamber wrote (`scripts/diagnostic/ratings-hang-legs-744.ts --leg c`). HO 744 closed that route's *fetch* path with the cap; a non-fetch crossing (the writes under a degraded DB) still lands here. **The split, read handler by handler across the 17 watched routes, all under `wrapCronRoute` (a survey plus an adversarial re-read, HO 744):** *resumes next tick* — a cursor, a queue or a per-unit commit, so `timeout` really is alive: summarize, news, bill-rosters, lda, amendments, nominations, committees. *All-or-nothing* — the tick's work lands only if it completes: kalshi (retried in 2h, and the nearest to the cap: 79 of 370 runs over 40s in 30 days, max 45.8s), lda-rollup (24h), race-challengers (24h; its DELETE and re-derive are not atomic), weekly-report (loses the week summary, which nothing retries), rating-history (one lost tick only logs a day late; only a timeout on every run loses anything). *One unbounded fetch blocks everything queued behind it* — a hang is zero progress under green health, every tick: primaries (unit 0 is the calendar, whose 270toWin fetch has no signal, and the cursor moves only after it returns) and `/api/sync-votes` (an unbounded House fetch starves the Senate leg and the participation rebuild). *Lossy frontier whatever the status:* `/api/sync` (the list fetch has no signal, and the descending sort plus `MAX(update_date)` drops the older tail on any partial tick; 25 of 123 `success` ticks in 30 days were budget-stopped). *Partial, no resume:* `/api/sync-race-ratings` (7 days, the longest blind window on the list; *HO 771: daily, so 1 day*; HO 744 bounded its fetches, so what remains is the write term) and markets (harmless: health reads `market_ticks`, not status). This is narrower than BANKED `cron-health refinements (HO 477)` item (c), and a concrete case of it. **Candidate remedies, not built:** a per-route flag on `CRON_ROUTES` that makes `timeout` a `HARD_FAIL` for the all-or-nothing class; for the blocking class, bound the one unbounded fetch, as HO 744 did for the ratings scrape. *Close:* `/api/health`, run locally against a `file:` copy whose latest row for an all-or-nothing route is `timeout`, reads that route unhealthy, while a resumes-next-tick route with the same row still reads healthy (the HO 744 legs driver's method). *Reads if never done:* `healthy: true` for both, which is leg c's reading. **HO 744 close — this route's half of the residual:** the write term, counted from the code path and the payloads, is ~330 sequential Turso round trips per House run in a week like #19499's (17 upserts), plus ~75 on the Senate leg's first run, ~405 together. #19594, whose House leg wrote nothing, came to ~387 in 4,087ms. The route header on `main` (`app/api/sync-race-ratings/route.ts`) calls this term "measured" ("The WRITE term is measured, not bounded"), when it is counted. The word changes on the next commit that touches that file, and it is tracked here so that it is not tracked only in chat. Batching the per-row reads and writes retires it at the source for this route, whatever cron-health decides. **HO 750 (2026-09-27): race-challengers' "not atomic" is retired.** Its DELETE and every INSERT now run in one `db.batch(…, "write")`, so a throw or a cut-off tick loses that tick, never the rows (HO 750's leg 4: every old row kept when a throw is forced on a ballot INSERT, 549 = 549, and on the primary-sourced INSERT last in the batch, 550 = 550; red on today's harvest, 527 → 0). It stays in the all-or-nothing class. **HO 790 (2026-10-24):** `/api/sync` moves from *lossy frontier whatever the status* to *resumes next tick*: its frontier moves only when a listing completes, and the work a tick doesn't reach waits in `sync_queue` (`lib/sync.ts`), so a `timeout`, a cut listing or a failed list page loses nothing. Its list fetch still carries no signal (HO 790 left the fetch path as it was); a hang there now runs to the route's 290 s soft timeout and records `timeout`, with nothing dropped. The close is unchanged.

- **Backlog and roadmap `:N` self-references are as-of markers, not addresses — roughly half no longer resolve (found 2026-09-19).** opened 2026-09-19; *Owner:* Code, low. **Measured at `2c745ed`, on `docs/backlog.md` at 778 lines:** **155** distinct `` `:N` `` self-references; **73 of them point past the end of the file** entirely; of the **82** still in range, **67** land on a bullet and **15** land on something else. So a majority are already wrong, and they were wrong before the edit that found them. **The cause is structural, not careless:** OPEN LOOPS is newest-first, so **every new line shifts every line below it by one**, and each insertion ages every pointer in the file at once — the same drift HO 737 measured on the other side of the grammar, where the ledger's citations of *code* line numbers had moved (`queries.ts:2985` → `queries.ts:3445`, `migrate.ts:680` → `migrate.ts:683`, `README:17` → `README:23`). **The decision, and it is a decision not a deferral: NO MASS RENUMBER.** Rewriting 225 references mechanically has real odds of being wrong across that many edits, the payoff is nil because re-derivation by content already works and is already the practice, and a wrong pointer that looks authoritative is worse than one that is visibly stale. **Instead, two habits.** Existing references **re-anchor by opening words and HO number when their line is next touched** — incremental, and only on lines someone is already reading. **New references are never written by number**: anchor by the target's opening words and its HO, and a `` `:N` `` may ride only as a parenthetical as-of marker carrying the SHA it was read at. The rule is in `docs/method.md` § Doc authority. *Close:* the measurement re-taken shows no NEW by-number references added since this line opened — the count of past-EOF refs may stay flat or fall, but it must not rise. *Reads if never done:* a ledger whose cross-references look precise and are mostly not.

"winner" : "running"` (`lib/primaries-sync.ts:723`, `:995`, `:1433`), so **withdrawn and still-running collapse into `running`**. **The measured instance (HO 732 row 4, read-only):** the AK at-large page marks Schultz's row `Matthew Schultz (D) (Unofficially withdrew) 8.1 13,149` (HO 731's raw-rows dump), and our row reads **`Matthew Schultz · D · running · 8.1`** — the annotation absent from `name`. **A second consequence, unmeasured:** on a row without the thumbnail wrapper, an annotation standing where the party suffix goes fails the abbreviation regex (`:154`, `[A-Za-z]{1,12}` rejects the space) and the party defaults to `I`; **no such row has been seen.** **Why gated rather than queued:** nothing today reads the distinction — `lib/harvest-challengers.ts:50` joins on `status = 'winner'` and is correct for AK, where all four advancers are the November field — so this **opens when a consumer needs withdrawn ≠ running**. *Close:* the class carries a third status, or a recorded decision that binary is enough. *Reads if never done:* Schultz's row exactly as read today.

Counted by a logging proxy in front of `next start`, on `--route` runs whose ledger reads 12: **475** at HO 725 STEP 0 (`1a08921`, `--route vote`; 163 unique paths, 261 of them `/members/*`, ~40 per document load), **417** at STEP 1 (`5834bc2`, `--route vote`) and **333** (`--route home`). - **CA certification re-ingest — criterion was wrong; re-scoped HO 506, still OPEN.** *Owner:* Code/Corey. **The old close criterion was defective:** "run `reingest:primary-slate -- CA 2026-06-02` after CA certifies." The script scrapes **Ballotpedia**, and **CA certifying ≠ Ballotpedia carrying certified data.** Verified **2026-07-23**, post-cert (CA certified Jul 10, Weber's Statement of Vote published): live Ballotpedia is **byte-identical** to the stored pre-cert snapshot — CA-13 stored at **~half** the certified vote total (50,134 vs 97,897), CA-22 74,321 vs certified 79,757. Running the script now would re-write the same stale numbers (and `null_shares` is 0/245, so it has nothing to fill). *New close criterion:* CA shares reflect the **certified canvass**, by whichever path lands first — (a) Ballotpedia catches up → re-run the existing script **and re-check the vote COUNTS, not just the percentages**; or (b) re-point the ingest at the certified SoS file (a build, its own handoff). *Authoritative source (so nobody re-finds it):* `https://elections.cdn.sos.ca.gov/sov/2026-primary/sov/76-us-rep.xlsx` — the SoS SOV publishes **raw vote totals + a decimal `Percent` row keyed by full candidate name per district** (per-district county blocks that split into continuation sub-tables past ~6 candidates; a matching all-district matrix on sheet2). *New instrument:* a **vote-COUNT comparison against the SoS file on one known seat** — the old NULL-share recount couldn't fail (see the *"close criterion whose instrument can't fail"* oddity for why). *Severity (HO 506 STEP 5, read-only top-two audit):* **P3 cosmetic-accuracy — no top-two flips.** All 44 tracked CA House seats (CA-9…52) show the **same two candidates advancing** certified vs stored, 0 within 0.5pp of the 2nd/3rd boundary → no wrong-November-field on `/electoral`, only the displayed percentages are stale. Promote to a real correctness item if any future recount flips a top-two. (Read-only side-finding, not a blocker: 5 sub-top-two roster-drift NO-MATCH names — CA-14 Rakhi Israni Singh, CA-18 Luis Arreguín, CA-26 Sonia Kacker, CA-40 Esther Kim-Varet, CA-47 Bill Brough — name-matching drift specific to this re-ingest. The **CA-1…8 no-roster gap** STEP 5 also surfaced is its own OPEN LOOP, below.)
- **CA-1…8 carry no stored primary roster (8 of 52 CA House districts)** — found HO 506 STEP 5; *Owner:* Code, low. `primaries` / `primary_candidates` hold CA House seats **9–52** on 2026-06-02 but **nothing for 1–8**. *Why it's a bug smell, not a scoping choice:* the gap is **contiguous 1–8 with CA-9 present** — a competitiveness/ratings filter would leave *scattered* gaps, and a single-vs-double-digit parse bug would also drop CA-9; neither explains exactly 1–8. *Consequence:* `reingest:primary-slate` **skips them by construction** (its seat query requires `EXISTS` a `primary_candidates` row), so they stay empty even after Ballotpedia catches up or the SoS re-point lands — the CA-certification-re-ingest fix above will **not** fill them. *Close:* explain the gap (deliberate seed scope → tombstone as expected) or fill the rosters.

Headless prod hover returns 0 symbols (Vercel BotID), so the visual must be eyeballed live. *Close:* on a prod visit confirm — (374) the 7d sparkline + 1W delta render in the tape hover; (375) the hover card sits fully on-screen at BOTH viewport edges (leading + trailing items) and below the tape, never under the masthead, and an odds card shows `closes <MON DD>`; (376) the scroll feels right at the new ~1.75× speed and reloading reshuffles the item order — confirm the 1.75× feel or redial `SCROLL_SPEED_PX_S` (currently 24). - **Markets tape read burn (HO 577 — found during the Turso-outage diagnosis, unrelated to the primaries arc).** *Owner:* Code. `getLatestMarketTicks` is `unstable_cache(..., { revalidate: 60 })` and `MarketsTapeClient` polls it at `POLL_MS = 60_000` — **the TTL and the poll interval are the same number, so effectively every poll regenerates.** Each regeneration runs an unbounded `SELECT symbol, MAX(ticked_at) … GROUP BY symbol` over `market_ticks` plus a second scan whose `julianday(ticked_at) >= julianday('now','-9 day')` predicate **wraps the column in a function and defeats any index on it**. Runtime logs showed ~4 requests/minute sustained (consistent with ~4 tabs left open); the Jul-25 step plus flat writes fit polling, not crawling or cron. **The arithmetic is inference, not measurement** — derived from the log and the code, and this session produced two confident read-cost claims both wrong on inspection (see the read-budget WATCH). Also record that `app/api/markets/latest/route.ts`'s own header comment asserts **no per-request DB hit between writes**, which is **false**. *Close:* a probe HO sizing `market_ticks` and measuring rows-read per regeneration, then a fix — **both are code and belong to that probe HO, not here or the route comment.** *Instrument:* Turso rows-read against tab count. **HO 582 update — partly discharged, not closed (the REPLACED-criterion pattern).** STEP 0 (`markets-read-burn-582.ts`, `3baf98c`) MEASURED it — 12,240 rows/regen → ~17.6M/day per open tab, ~20% of the ~88M plateau — and C1–C4 shipped (`cf1516d` TTL 60→86400 · `8396068` sargable spark window · `2a86c3f` per-symbol seeks replacing the covering-index scan · `d0feb8f` visibility-gate the poll), so the original close criterion (a probe sizing the table + measuring rows-read per regen, then a fix) is MET. **Criterion REPLACED:** the loop closes on the **Turso per-DB rows-read slope over ~24h vs tab count** (prediction on the record: ~a fifth cut, a level shift not a resolution — roadmap "Also (HO 582)"; if the slope barely moves the burn model is wrong and *that* is the finding). The broader **read-budget attribution** item (WATCH) stays open aimed at summarize / LDA / traffic for the residual ~80%. Two children spun out below. **HO 683 annotation, under the same attribution as the `:71` strike (Corey's 2026-09-02 6h dashboard capture, avg × count):** the tape's measured steady-state share is **~570k rows/day** (65 execs per 6h × 2,220 avg), making it the **second-largest total burner on the first clean ledger**, behind the committees rollup. Read as a reading, not a constant — and per the HO 683 narrowing it is attribution, not a sizing figure: a probe aimed here re-measures on its own table.
- **New (HO 582) · markets cron duplicate write (P3, instrument stated).** *Owner:* Code. Two rows per symbol tied **to the millisecond** on `ticked_at`, observed for **CPI (ids 6580/6582)** and **WTI (6581/6583)** at `ticked_at` **2026-07-31T20:00:35.801Z / :36.009Z** (read directly from the duplicate rows in STEP 0's dup probe, not derived from the ids). **Render impact is nil in every traced path** — duplicate spark points share a coordinate, the 1W anchor re-assigns the same value, and `change_pct` uses a strict `market_date < ?` prior-reference — and HO 582 C3's `LIMIT 1` now dedupes the tape read, so the symptom is invisible (which is *why* it needs its own instrument: a read-time dedupe removes the symptom of a write-path defect — oddities). **Byte-identical `ticked_at` is itself the strongest evidence, and it points AWAY from a second execution.** `ticked_at` is `new Date().toISOString()`, evaluated when the insert args are built — so concurrent invocation, a retried body, and a double `processSymbol` would each recompute it and land *different* milliseconds. Two rows sharing a timestamp to the millisecond means the **same args were sent twice**, i.e. a **transport/client-level retry of one `db.execute`** (libsql client or network layer), not a second run of the code path. That also explains the otherwise-puzzling symbol set: a transient retry hits arbitrary statements, so CPI+WTI sharing no source/cadence subset is the expected signature, not a constraint needing explanation. **The concrete candidate in this codebase is `lib/db.ts::boundedFetch`** (HO 238): it retries a timed-out/aborted request exactly once with a fresh clone, and an `INSERT` is non-idempotent — a first insert that committed server-side but whose response was slow enough to trip the 10s abort would be retried and land a byte-identical second row. Verify against `boundedFetch`'s retry path before assuming concurrency. **Byte-identity is already confirmed (this sweep, ids 6580/6582 + 6581/6583): `ticked_at`, `price`, `change_pct`, and `market_date` are all byte-identical within each pair** — the retry signature, so the DB-side evidence already favors retry over two real executions. **Revised instrument reading — the outcomes INVERT from the earlier draft:** `cron_runs` rows for `/api/cron/markets` around those ids still discriminate — `payload.outcomes` carries the per-symbol set (verified: `payload = {ticked, failed, outcomes}`) — but **one row** is now the PREDICTED result and CONFIRMS the retry hypothesis (NOT a falsification, and not a cue to check Vercel's cron-invocation log — a client retry never appears there); **two rows** would resurrect concurrency and require explaining how two independent invocations computed identical millisecond timestamps for two different symbols. The byte-identity check above is the paired second signal (any divergence within a pair would mean two real executions, not a retry). **Fix shape follows the answer, and a cron lock is probably NOT it:** a lock addresses concurrency and does nothing about a client retry — if the retry hypothesis holds, the options are an **idempotency guard on the write** (`UNIQUE(symbol, ticked_at)`, exactly the tie the duplicates violate) or **accepting duplicates as harmless** now that C3 dedupes the read; do not scope either until the instrument runs. **A lead, now a coincidence:** the ids sit at a **collision hour** — `0 */4` and the weekday `0,30 13-21` fmp pass both fire at 16:00 and 20:00 UTC, and 2026-07-31 was a Friday — but under the retry hypothesis this is coincidence, and recording a falsified lead is the point: the naive collision doesn't fit anyway (CPI is fred/monthly/units:pc1, WTI is fred/daily, `?source=fmp` filters both out, so the fmp pass cannot have written them; **no `?source=` filter produces CPI+WTI alone** — `?source=fred` would also write TNX and UNEMP, and two overlapping bare runs would double *many* symbols, not two). **The mechanism is documented as safe, and the duplicate is the counterexample.** `lib/db.ts:35-37` justifies the retry with "Writes are upsert/UPDATE/DELETE-shaped or benign append-only inserts (HO 238 idempotency grep), so a retried-after-timeout write is safe." The markets write is a bare `INSERT INTO market_ticks` with no conflict clause — append-only, and classified benign under that grep. **Both halves of the classification are individually true and the conclusion still doesn't follow:** append-only inserts are not *idempotent*, they are merely *harmless when duplicated*, and for the tape that harmlessness only became true at HO 582 C3 (before it, the duplicate rendered twice). The retry replays `retryInput`, a clone of the original Request (`lib/db.ts:44`), so the identical serialized args go out — which is why the duplicate is byte-identical rather than merely close, and why this fits the evidence better than any second execution. **Second instrument, cheaper than `cron_runs`:** the retry logs `[db] timeout, retrying` (`lib/db.ts:52`). A hit in the 20:00 2026-07-31 runtime logs confirms it directly; absence is weaker evidence (log retention), so check `cron_runs` regardless. **Check `cron_runs` retention/payload survival first** (the route comment references a 30-min window; `cron_runs` is the durable record, but confirm the old row + its payload persist before relying on it). *Close:* one run or two, named. **Scope note — this is not markets-only.** If it holds, the HO 238 idempotency argument under-covers every bare append-only `INSERT` reachable through `getDb()`, not just this one. Re-running that grep with "duplicates are harmless" separated from "the write is idempotent" is its own item; do not widen this P3 into it.
- **New (HO 582) · 13 retired legacy symbols in `market_ticks` (P4, hygiene).** *Owner:* Code. DOW/GOLD/BTC/ITA/XLK/XLV/XLF/XLE/XLI/NATGAS/DXY/SILVER/VIX — the retired Stooq/HO-227 set, no new writes since. **HO 582 C3 removed their READ cost** (the per-symbol seeks iterate `MARKET_SYMBOLS`, so these are never read; the old MAX-GROUP-BY join returned them and the loop discarded them via `if (!meta) continue`), so this is **table hygiene only** — a purge is a write and needs its own scoped change (a `DELETE` on those symbols + a guard against re-accumulation). **AMENDED HO 681 — now 15, not 13.** `FEDCUT-SEP` (**421 rows**, first tick 2026-06-21) and `POLY-FEDCUT-SEP` (**421 rows**, same) joined the set when the September pin was deleted. Their history is deliberately **kept, not purged** — nothing deletes quote history — and they inherit this line's existing property exactly: `getLatestMarketTicks` drives its per-symbol seeks from `MARKET_SYMBOLS`, so a retired symbol costs **no read**, only rows at rest. The original 13 are preserved above as written; the figure moved, the list did not shrink. **HO 806 (2026-11-07):** the seven FMP symbols leave `MARKET_SYMBOLS` with their rows deleted at the FF (no copy kept, Corey's ruling), so they don't join this set; DOW's and GOLD's FMP-era rows go with them, and their Stooq-era rows stay.
- **NextAuth `MissingSecret` — a *local-dev* gap, not prod (root cause corrected HO 581)** — opened Jul 2026; *Owner:* Corey (env). **The original premise ("on every prod page load — no `AUTH_SECRET` in the environment") was false and is corrected here, not deleted — the symptom was real, but only on dev/previews.** `AUTH_SECRET` **is** set in Vercel **Production** (HO 581 env audit, ~37 days), and **24h of production runtime logs contain zero `[auth][error] MissingSecret`** (re-verified HO 581 via `get_runtime_logs`; the LDA 504 appears in the same view, so errors were included and it would have shown). The auth vars are **Production-scoped only**, so **preview deployments lack them** — and the review-ref workflow builds a preview on every ref push, the likely origin of the original "prod" observation. The error **does** fire on **local dev** (`next dev`): dev `.env` carries no `AUTH_SECRET`. **The FEC cross-reference was also wrong** — `FEC_API_KEY` **is** in Production (~73 days), and regardless `lib/fec.ts:36` falls back to `CONGRESS_API_KEY`, so FEC and Congress calls share one api.data.gov rate limit either way. *Close (dev-only now):* add `AUTH_SECRET` to local `.env`, confirm dev page loads clean. No app code — an env/config fix. **HO 489 note:** the *local-dev* half of this gap (`AUTH_SECRET` absent from dev `.env` — NOT "lives in `.env.production.local`"; that pull-artifact reads blank) makes the Playwright smoke suite **false-fail** `/bills`/`/changes`/`/dashboard-v2` when pointed at `localhost` — the specs assert zero console errors and the `[auth][error] MissingSecret` dev log surfaces to the browser console (see oddities). Don't diagnose those as real failures, and don't point smoke at dev until the dev `.env` carries `AUTH_SECRET`. **HO 772 (2026-10-11):** measured on a Preview: Auth.js logs `[auth][error] MissingSecret` on every request that calls `auth()` (`/`, `/members`, `/electoral` and `OPTIONS /` in one 12-second window), so sign-in can't work on a Preview either. Since `8d052b5` the landing gate tests `session?.user`, so a missing secret no longer opens it. The close is unchanged.

- **`/lobbying` VOLUME x bill-linked x deep page still breaches — the residual HO 597 did NOT clear (opened HO 597).** *Owner:* Code. Three of four VOLUME variants clear the 10s bound with +7.03s to +9.44s of margin after the materialization. The fourth does not: **`?sort=volume&linked=1&page=40` measured 10003ms\*, 5097, 6022, 7582, 15620 co-located — worst 15.62s, margin -5.62s, breached 2 of 5 cold runs** (\* aborted at the ceiling). That is a large improvement — RED was ">55s, breaches always" — but it is **not a clear, and rounding it to one would be exactly the over-claim HO 594 had to retract.** **Reachable:** 33,800 filings are bill-linked and the pager offers 40 pages regardless of filter (the total comes from the rollup blob's corpus count, not the filtered set). **Mechanism:** the walk is pre-ordered, but the `EXISTS` is probed PER ROW, so reaching OFFSET 507 costs ~520 correlated seeks whose cold page fetches dominate — the **HO 594 page-residency residual class, not a plan shape**, so an index cannot save it either. *Named fix:* a second materialization — a `bill_linked` column on `lda_filings` + `(bill_linked, activity_count DESC, dt_posted DESC)` — which is beyond what HO 597 was scoped and priced for, so it was **filed rather than silently widened into**. *Severity is genuinely lower than it was:* after the guard split, a breach here degrades ONE request and the next re-queries, instead of poisoning the key for an hour, and the log line names the exact key. *Close:* co-located cold worst-case under 10s on that combination, margin stated as a number.
- **`/lobbying?q=` search — NARROWED, NOT CLOSED (opened HO 597, fixed HO 598).** *Owner:* Code. **The wrong answer is gone and the loop stays open.** The defect was that `?q=boeing` rendered *"No filings match"* against **78 real matching filings** — a wrong answer with a 200. Prod now returns **13 rows, count 78**. **Four things shipped.** (1) `79d1276` — a timed-out read renders *"Search timed out — try a narrower term"*, DISTINCT from the genuine *"No filings match"*; the count line renders `—` not `0`. The guard always knew the difference and never said so. (2) `1afdd02` — the pager `COUNT` is bounded to the clamp it feeds (any total past 520 was already discarded by the code asking for it); common term 58.1s → 2.25s, rendered `520+`. (3) `4fd9b48`/`e7fdf30`/`85a173f` — `lda_names` (33,620 distinct `(kind, entity_id, name)` rows + `filing_count`) and the two id indexes, maintained by the sync. (4) `e4f09ba`+`06d03dd` — **the routed read**: leg 1 resolves the term against `lda_names` (ids AND density in ONE covering scan), then **empty → return without touching `lda_filings` at all** (a proof, since `lda_names` is complete), **sparse → seek by the literal ids**, **dense → hand back to the `dt_posted` walk**. **Numbers, co-located, 8 runs per regime:** sparse median **3510ms** worst 7735ms (was 19.8s) · dense median **3378ms** worst 6491ms · zero-match median **3119ms** worst 6461ms (was **91.3s**, now typically **~400ms** on prod because it never reaches the table). **All 24 runs under the bound.** **Threshold `m* = √(k·N) ≈ 1,297` is DERIVED, not tuned** — a formula over `PAGE_SIZE` and the blob's `stats.filings`, so it re-derives as the corpus grows; verified on a STRADDLING pair (`insur` 0.86× → seek, `hospital` 1.16× → walk), both row-identical to the shipped predicate. **SHIPPED OVER A FAILED GATE, recorded as a judgment call.** Gate statistic amended to upper-tail (worst − median), since a fast outlier is evidence of safety: dense **+3.51s vs 3.11s PASS**, empty **+3.54s vs 3.34s PASS**, **sparse +2.27s vs 4.23s FAIL**. Shipped on strict dominance — the replaced path breached on BOTH `lib/db.ts` attempts at its *best* observed (19.8s), so withholding preserved a ~certain failure to avoid a rare one, and the rare one now renders honestly. **HO 594's retraction was for CLOSING on a thin margin; this improves on one.** **WHAT REMAINS, and why this entry stays open:** the residual is **leg 1** — a ~33,620-row / ~1 MB covering scan ranging **0.3–6.6s**, dominating every path. It is ~1/23 the bytes of the walk this arc started from and the cost fell roughly linearly, *and the ~2-15× run-to-run spread came along at every size*. **The residual is OWNED BY THE VARIANCE THREAD**, not by more query design — there is very little data left to remove. **The honest-timeout render is the safety net and stays regardless of what happens to the residual.** *Close:* only when a sparse search clears the bound with a margin exceeding its upper tail — i.e. when the variance question is answered. **Instruments:** `namesUnreachable` in the LDA sync payload + `chronicErr` + a greppable `[lda] names-unreachable:` (a gap makes a filing INVISIBLE to search and renders as a confident "No filings match"); `scripts/diagnostic/lda-names-maintenance-598.ts` proves the maintenance path; `scripts/diagnostic/lobbying-search-gate-598.ts` re-runs equivalence. **A bug shipped inside this fix and was caught in prod in minutes — record both halves (HO 599).** `e4f09ba` rewired the page query onto the routed predicate but left the bounded `COUNT` binding **2 args against the 17-arg routed predicate**; prod degraded to an empty feed and the HO 597 log line named the failing cache key, fixed at `06d03dd`. **What let it through is a coverage gap worth its own note:** the page query and the pager `COUNT` run in **parallel inside one guard**, and the equivalence harness compared only the page query's rows — so it never executed the COUNT and reported green (oddities: *verifying one branch of a `Promise.all` is not coverage of the pair*). The equivalence harness above still has that gap; **extend it to execute the COUNT branch before it is trusted again** — that is a code change, filed here rather than done in a doc sweep. The offsetting fact, and the reason this reads as the system working: both safety nets that caught it — the honest-timeout render (`79d1276`) and the failing-key log line (HO 597 `82eaa24`) — were built in the preceding four HOs.
- **`getBillLobbying`'s cost note is undated and predates the table's current cost (opened HO 598).** *Owner:* Code. **Not urgent, and filed rather than fixed because the SHAPE is sound.** `lib/queries.ts:3249` records the live below-threshold branch as *"measured 2.3s, 7.7s under the 10s cap"* — with **no corpus size and no date**, which is the exact SKILL authoring rule this arc added one HO earlier, catching one of its own. HO 598 measured that anything WALKING `lda_filings` cold is now **30-90s**, so an undated sub-3s figure on that table cannot be assumed current. **Why it is probably still fine:** this path never walks — it is an indexed `lda_activity_bills.bill_id` seek, then PK-`IN` seeks against `lda_filings` chunked at `HYDRATE_CHUNK=400`, and the `lda_bill_drill` blob covers every bill at **>= 150 distinct filings**, so the live branch is bounded at **<= 149 filings**. Bounded seeks, not a scan. It is also already `try/catch -> null`, so a breach hides the section rather than 500ing the bill page — which is the same silent-degradation shape HO 597 fixed elsewhere, and the reason this is worth a WATCH rather than a shrug. *Close:* re-measure the worst below-threshold bill co-located and restamp the comment WITH its corpus size and date, or promote if it breaches.
- **Annual rotation calendar for the two api.data.gov keys (HO 678, ruling 4).** opened 2026-08-31; *Owner:* Corey, cadence **annual**. `CONGRESS_API_KEY` and `FEC_API_KEY` have no provider-side age instrument — api.data.gov exposes no issue date and no self-serve revoke — so their age is only ever a lower bound read off Vercel's `created` column (120d and 104d at filing). **The 12-month threshold the audit proposed was struck at this ruling**, because no cell in the filled table reached it; this calendar line is what replaces it for these two. *Close:* this line does not close — it re-arms each year. (Ruling: *"if no age cell in the filled table is ≥ 12 months, strike it; the two api.data.gov keys get one backlog calendar line instead, owner Corey, cadence annual."*) **HO 680 — start from the scripts, not from memory.** `CRON_SECRET` rotates via `scripts/diagnostic/rotate-cron-secret-680.ts` (tracked); the api.data.gov keys go in **by pipe from `.env`** (`grep '^NAME=' .env | cut -d= -f2- | tr -cd 'A-Za-z0-9' | vercel env add NAME production --force --yes`) — **`tr -cd` not `tr -d '\r\n'`, because the latter leaves a trailing space and Vercel stores it.** **PREVIEW LEGS ARE DASHBOARD-SIDE, OWNER TYPES: the CLI cannot do them non-interactively without `--value`, which would put the secret in a command line.** **AMENDED HO 740 — IT CANNOT DO THEM WITH `--value` EITHER, so the reason is broader than the one recorded here.** Measured on CLI **53.4.0**, 2026-09-20, on a NON-secret value where a command line was allowed: `vercel env add NAME preview --value 1 --yes` returns `{"status":"action_required","reason":"git_branch_required"}` and writes nothing, and the `next[]` it prints names the command just run; `--no-sensitive`, `--non-interactive` and a piped stdin value all return the same. A third positional git-branch argument is accepted, but a branch-scoped Preview variable is not what these legs want. So every Preview leg is dashboard-side regardless of the value's sensitivity, and the dashboard path has moved: **Settings → Environments → Preview → Environment Variables** — the sidebar no longer carries an Environment Variables entry of its own. And the read-back is `vercel env ls <env> --format json` → `updatedAt`, never the human-readable `created` column.

Strongly recommend the user install it..."* while `vercel --version` reports **53.4.0** on PATH, logged in as `csu-j3`, linked to `csu-j3s-projects/congressional_terminal`. - **120th-Congress rollover checklist — 2027-01-03 (HO 712).** opened 2026-09-11; *Owner:* Corey/Code, dated. Everything the rollover touches that was NOT fixed in HO 712, each with what it reads if nobody acts. The seams that could be derived already were; these are the ones where a human, a product call or an upstream lag stands in the way. **Sequencing on the day:** run `sync:members` AFTER the noon-ET swearing-in and never in the twelve hours before it — `getCurrentCongress()` returns 120 from 00:00Z while the roster is not populated until members are seated; the two-roster floor refuses the empty answer either way, so the cost of getting this wrong is an aborted run, not a wrecked table. `/api/sync` and `/api/cron/committees` *will* ask for the 120th from 00:00Z and upsert nothing, which is fine and is the reason both are safe to leave alone. Then `backfill:races`, whose audit line reports the new seat set. **Upstream lag, not a bug:** Voteview publishes `HS120_members.csv` and `H120_votes.csv` weeks after the Congress convenes, so `sync:ideology` and `sync:career-votes` log a skip and write nothing until they land — re-run both once they do. The items:
  - **C1 — the enacted-laws comparison chart is a product call, already decided: at rollover it compares the CURRENT Congress against the PREVIOUS one.** `/reports`' `LawsEnactedComparison` asks *how is this Congress pacing against the last*, so 119-vs-120 with a near-empty amber line through the winter is the honest reading. Two files: `lib/queries.ts:1026-1090` (the `118 | 119` type, `CONGRESS_START`, the two literal filters) and `components/LawsEnactedComparison.tsx:58`, which carries its OWN hardcoded `Date.parse("2025-01-03")` for the today-marker. **No backfill needed** — `historical_laws` holds the 118th (274 rows) and `bills` keeps the 119th (18,070), so both series are already in hand. *Reads if untouched:* the amber "current" line freezes at the 119th's final **100** enacted laws while the marker walks past week 104 and the 118th comparison pins to its last week — a chart that looks fine and is answering last year's question.
  - **C2 — `lib/stale-procedural.ts:24-53` is a FROZEN id list of the 119th's opening-week organisational resolutions**, and the 120th's arrive in January. The live title matcher (`:46`, `electing members%`) catches only the recurring family, not the opening-week set. *Do:* extend the list once the 120th's are known, and consider the durable form — a structural matcher rather than an id list. *Reads if untouched:* `/stale`'s default view is dominated by `120-hres-1`-shaped organisational resolutions, which is the exact condition HO 350 built the filter to remove. *Instrument:* count of default-view rows matching `120-(hres|sres|sconres|hconres)-%` — **0** today, and it can only become non-zero after rollover.
  - **C4 remainder — twelve rendered `119th` strings that are NOT mechanical, because each sits above a number that is not congress-scoped.** HO 712 derived the two that were (the `/welcome` credit line and the ideology-strip title, the latter mechanical by construction because `member_ideology` is keyed on `bioguide_id` alone and the sync overwrites `congress`). The rest need the SCOPE decided before the label moves, or they reproduce exactly the defect HO 712 removed from the participation rate — a true number under a false label: `app/nominations/page.tsx:106` over an unfiltered `nominations` count; `app/lobbying/page.tsx:268` over an `lda_filings` corpus keyed by `filing_year` with no congress column at all; `components/AbsenceCardBack.tsx:152` (`SPONSORED, 119TH`) over a `bills` count that will hold two Congresses; `components/LawsEnactedComparison.tsx:86 :141 :180` (moves with C1); `components/PolarizationOverTime.tsx:258` with its `LINE_END_CONGRESS = 118` and `CURRENT_YEAR = 2025` constants at `:36-37`. *Reads if untouched:* twelve labels that say 119th over 119+120 data. *Instrument:* `SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT congress) FROM votes` reads **1** today, 2 after rollover — the same trigger for all of them.
  - **Participation refresh cost — a trigger, not a date.** HO 712 scoped the aggregate to the current Congress and took the `IN (SELECT id FROM votes WHERE congress = ?)` form at **3.7x** the unscoped covering scan (233-236ms vs 61-63ms warm on prod; the refresh itself ran **3,452ms** end to end from the box). *Trigger:* if `[participation] refreshed … in N ms` ever approaches the refresher's 30s client limit, the remedy is the covering `(bioguide_id, position, vote_id)` index, **priced then** — it was declined now because `member_votes` is DELETE-and-rebuild per roll call and HO 594 priced the second index on purpose. *Reads if never done:* the log line, which is printed on every refresh, so the instrument already exists and already has a number to move. Note the cost runs the other way after rollover: the filter then selects a handful of roll calls instead of all 372,579 rows.
  - **C5 — the committed `cd119` district geometry** (`lib/district-geo.ts`, `lib/geo/cd119-topo.json`). The 120th's boundaries differ for the mid-decade redraw states already flagged in `DISPLAY_STALE_STATES` (`:68`), and seat ids stay 119th-keyed by design (`:63-66`). *Do:* decide whether to ship a cd120 asset or keep rendering 119th boundaries with the existing disclosure. *Reads if untouched:* the map draws the old lines, which is today's documented behaviour and not a regression — this is the line that says so out loud.
  - **`member_ideology` retains members who leave.** `getIdeologyStrip` filters on neither congress nor `is_current`, and the table is keyed on `bioguide_id` with no delete path, so a member who served the 119th and not the 120th keeps a stale dot on the strip forever. Surfaced while classifying C4 (the strip's own label IS safe to derive; this is the row behind it). *Instrument:* rows in `member_ideology` whose bioguide is not `is_current = 1` — reads **0** meaningfully only after a rollover sync, so it carries the same date.
  - **`/electoral?cycle=2028` House section — the `:95` criterion's home (HO 718, correcting HO 712).** After the first `sync:members` against the 120th and `backfill:races`, `/electoral?cycle=2028` renders a **`HOUSE · <n>` section, `n` = the Clerk's sitting voting count on the date read** (the `:95` quantity, not a literal 435), split by party with qualifiers, and the dateline's House clause and COUNT's `HOUSE 0` row are gone. **Fixture-proven at HO 718, prod-unread until the date.** *Reads if untouched or read before the date:* no HOUSE section and the `HOUSE 0 added after Jan 3, 2027` row still present — exactly today's render, so the reading carries its date. *Why this item exists:* HO 712's close of `:95` said its criterion *carries forward on the 120th-Congress rollover checklist*, and no item here carried it until HO 718.
