## QUEUED

- **New (HO 786) · FILED, data integrity — two indexes prod has and `scripts/migrate.ts` doesn't make, so a database it builds answers 500 on `/stale`.** opened 2026-10-19; *Owner:* Code. Prod's `sqlite_master` defines `CREATE INDEX idx_bills_dash_stage ON bills(is_ceremonial, stage, update_date)` and `CREATE INDEX idx_bills_dash_topics ON bills(is_ceremonial, topics)` (read 2026-10-19; SKILL's schema lists both, HO 241), but `migrate.ts` creates neither; its comments name them (`:1534`, `:1618`). `lib/queries.ts:7311` (`getStaleCommitteeCount`, `/stale`'s footer count) forces the first by `INDEXED BY`, so on a database `migrate.ts` built, `/stale` answers 500 with *no such index: idx_bills_dash_stage* (HO 786's legs, on both builds). The second has no `INDEXED BY` site, so its absence changes plans, not answers. *Fix:* both in `migrate.ts` as `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`, an expand step. *Close:* a database built by `migrate.ts` alone carries both, and `/stale` answers 200 on it. *Reads if never done:* every `file:` copy of prod, and any rebuild from `migrate.ts`, answers 500 on `/stale`.
- **New (HO 785) · FILED, instrument family — gitleaks at its default archive depth doesn't read inside the handoff archives.** opened 2026-10-18; *Owner:* Code, standing. HO 678's repo-wide gitleaks scan does not read inside `docs/handoffs-archive-*.tar.gz` at its default archive depth (HO 785, measured: 0 at the default, the same two fixture strings at depth 2); any scan that claims coverage of an archive runs with the archive depth at 2 or over the extracted set, and every future archive is scanned extracted before it is committed, as HO 785 was.

- **New (HO 684) · FILED — the rating-source short-label map now exists in FOUR places, and none of them is the source of truth.** *Owner:* Code, low. `rating_history.source` / `race_ratings.source` store **snake_case** (`cook` / `sabato` / `inside_elections`), and four separate sites turn that into something a human reads: `lib/enums.ts:214` `RACE_RATING_SOURCES` (full names — "Cook Political Report") · `components/RatingChip.tsx:19-28` `sourceLabel` (title case — "Inside Elections") · `components/RaceCard.tsx:180-184` the `raterPills` array (short caps — `COOK` / `SABATO` / `IE`, an inline literal, not a constant) · and now `components/RaceMovedIndicator.tsx` `SOURCE_SHORT` (the same short caps, for the MOVED chip). **HO 684 added the fourth deliberately and did not extract**, on the ruling that the HO was the badge referent and not a label-map refactor — this line is that decision made visible rather than left in a comment. *Two things to carry:* the four are **not redundant** (three genuinely different registers — full, title, short — for three surfaces), so the fix is a single keyed map with three fields, **not** one string; and there is no failing test today, so this is tidiness, not a defect. *Close:* one source-of-truth map consumed by all four, or a tombstone ruling the duplication acceptable.

- **New (HO 683) · FILED — the committees rollup is the largest total burner on the first trustworthy ledger.** *Owner:* Code. `getCommitteesIndex`'s two LEFT JOIN subqueries (per-committee `member_count` + 30-day `recent_count`) read **~247k rows per 6h** — 30,900 avg × 8 executions — the top line on the 2026-09-02 6h window and roughly 10% of the extrapolated ~2.5M/day. It is the **first legitimate optimization candidate under the HO 683 narrowing** (probes may be AIMED at attributed lines), and it is **filed, not chased**. Two constraints on whoever picks it up. **The figure is attribution, not sizing:** per the same narrowing, no Turso-window number is ground truth until `:427` reconciles, so a probe **re-measures on its own table** before proposing anything — and `EXPLAIN` prices the plan, not the scan, so that measurement is a timed, co-located, WORST-CASE reading rather than a median (SKILL, Query helpers). **And the surface is not `/committees`:** that route redirects to `/members`, where this query backs the LEFT RAIL on every render, so any change is a `/members` change and inherits its consumers. Any probe is its own HO.


- **New (HO 681) · a SECOND Fed-cut horizon, as a next-after-next ROLLER — fork B, filed rather than built.** *Owner:* **Corey**, to want or strike. **This is a FEATURE, not the duplicate fix** — HO 681 deleted the September pin and shipped a single horizon; two horizons was the pin's original purpose at HO 302 and keeping two *properly* means a real second roller ("the meeting after the soonest-open one"), not a pinned event ticker that goes stale the moment the roller reaches it. **A roller DISCOVERS; it does not pin** — so it would use neither `kalshiEvent` nor `polyMonth`, and re-adding a pin is re-creating the defect. Shape if wanted: extend the Kalshi discovery in `fetchKalshi` to select the **second** soonest-open event in the series (it already sorts them by date and takes `dated[0]`), and the Polymarket side to take the second `fed-decision-in-*` by end date. The HO 681 collision flag would then be the thing that proves the two horizons stay distinct, which is a good reason to build it on top of that HO rather than before it. *Close:* Corey rules want or strike.
- **New (HO 681) · `kalshiEvent` and `polyMonth` are carrier-less config fields — strike or keep, rides the next markets HO.** *Owner:* Code, low. Their only carriers were the two symbols HO 681 deleted, so **no symbol sets either today**; the READERS survive (`lib/markets.ts` `:415` for the Kalshi pin, `:510` for the Polymarket month) and were kept on purpose, with the comments reconciled to RECORD rather than orphaned — the `bareTape` shape from HO 669. **The keep reason is weaker than it looks and is stated so it can be weighed honestly:** fork B above, the one plausible future second horizon, is a roller and **would not use either field**, so "kept for a future pin" is kept for a use case nobody has named. **One behaviour goes with them if they go, and it should be recorded before it is:** the pinned Kalshi path derives its date from `parseTickerDate(kalshiEvent)`, whose **day defaults to 01** (`lib/markets.ts:418-421`), so `FEDCUT-SEP` stored `market_date 2026-09-01` for a meeting on **2026-09-16** for its whole life — never noticed because only the month is read downstream (`showMonth`). Any future pin inherits that. *Close:* strike both fields and the pinned branch in `fetchKalshi`, **or** record a named future carrier so the fields stop reading as dead.
- **The two "+N" overflow affordances in the expand panel have no destination (opened HO 675).** *Owner:* Code. The cosponsor faces cap at six and print a `+182` chip; the related-bills list caps at five and prints `+86 more`. **Both render as plain text rather than links, deliberately**, because the surfaces they would open do not exist: there is no cosponsor-roster page and `/bill/[id]` renders no related-bill list (it takes only `BillStageBar` from the panel module — `app/bill/[id]/page.tsx:5`). The mock draws both as `<a href="#">`, and shipping a mock's placeholder href is precisely how a link that goes nowhere gets built, so the affordance was rendered without the promise. **The cap is chosen so the promise is rarely needed:** at five, **98.24%** of bills with related rows show no overflow at all (measured: `<=2` 93.01% · `<=5` 98.24% · `<=10` 99.29% · max 91). The cosponsor chip is unavoidable — 61.4% of rosters are single-party and most exceed six. *Close:* either a destination exists and both become links (the natural one is a roster section on `/bill/[id]`, which would also give the related list somewhere to go), or a reason is recorded for leaving them inert — in both cases re-stating the overflow rate so the decision is made against the number.
- **The Cosponsors row — and therefore the HO 675 faces — does not render on `/watchlist` or `/members/[bioguideId]` (opened HO 675).** *Owner:* Code. The row is gated on `bill.cosponsor_count != null`, and that column arrives only through `SPONSOR_ENRICH_SELECT` (`lib/queries.ts:89`), which is used by `getNewBillsThisWeek`, `getFeedBills`, `getStaleBills` and `getStageChanges` — **not** by `getWatchlistBills` or `getMemberBills`, whose SELECT lists omit it. So the panel renders the faces on **five of the seven routes** it mounts on. The same omission also costs those two routes the sponsor portrait (`sponsor_depiction_url`), which is HO 673's shipped feature, so this is not new breakage — HO 675 inherited it and did not widen it. **Widening the gate was considered and declined at STEP 0:** with the column null there is no total to print above the groups, so the row would have to source its total from the roster, which is the `cosponsor_count` repair HO 675 was explicitly forbidden to make. *Close:* the two queries carry the enrichment (or the gate is re-cut on something both have), demonstrated by opening a panel on `/watchlist` and on a member hub and reading a populated Cosponsors row with faces — against the control that both read no row today.
- **A fourth party-bracket implementation (opened HO 675).** *Owner:* Code. `[D-NV-4]` is now built independently in four places: `components/BillExpandPanel.tsx:212` (`SponsorMeta`), `components/SponsorHoverName.tsx:108`, `components/V2FeedList.tsx:287` (abbreviated, no district), and `lib/bill-rosters-view.ts` (`toFace`, added by this HO). Three of the four apply the House-at-large `-AL` fallback; `V2FeedList`'s deliberately does not, because it renders a shorter form. **HO 675 added the fourth knowingly rather than unifying**, for the same reason HO 673 declined to unify the two `SponsorPhoto`s: the sponsor block's rendered markup is asserted byte-identical against a before-capture in that HO's gate, and rewriting how it computes an identical string is risk with no gain inside a render HO. *Close:* one implementation with the `-AL` rule and the abbreviated variant both expressed as options, and the other three call sites deleted — verified by rendering a House at-large member, a House member with a district, and a Senator through the surviving implementation and diffing each against its current output, which must be unchanged.
- **The promoted companion is not deduped against the HEARING block's agenda, and that is a ruling (opened HO 675).** *Owner:* Corey, if he disagrees. The mock's section 03 requires that a bill not print twice in one panel column, and HO 675 implements that for the "also related" list — filtered against the agenda of the one meeting the HEARING block chose (76 bills corpus-wide have such an overlap; control: 34,958 agenda sibling pairs exist, so the join discriminates). **The promoted block is exempt.** Suppressing a cross-chamber twin because it also happens to sit on today's markup agenda would erase the single fact that block exists to report — that the bill has a parallel track in the other chamber — and the mock does not cover the case. Flagged rather than taken silently. *Close:* Corey either ratifies the exemption or rules the opposite, in which case the filter moves up to cover `promoted` as well and the affected bills are re-counted.
- **Two branches of the roster refresh have never fired, and the reconcile is why (opened HO 677).** *Owner:* Code, low. **(a) The refresh's own `cosponsor_count` write.** HO 677 made `lib/bill-rosters-refresh.ts` the column's owner, but the one-shot reconcile in the same commit left **0 bills** where the column and `active_count` differ — so the branch has nothing to write and has not run in production. **(b) T1, the never-checked trigger.** Every one of the 17,729 bills carries a `bill_roster_state` row (the HO 676 seed covered the corpus), so the priority band's first rung reads 0 and has never selected a bill; HO 676's `119-hr-9641` was caught by count-ahead, not by T1. **Both are as close to exercised as they can get without a real divergence:** the count-write's UPDATE is the identical statement the reconcile ran **2,013** times, and a 5-bill `--write` slice over settled bills reported `cosponsor_count fixed: 0` while stamping all 5 — which proves the write is **conditional**, since an unconditional one would have reported 5. **The awkward part is worth stating: a good outcome is what hides them.** The reconcile removed the divergence and the seed removed the never-checked band, so the two branches most likely to be wrong the day they matter are the two nothing can currently trigger. *Triggers:* (a) fires on the next real roster divergence — a bill whose cosponsors move between checks; (b) fires on the first bill `/api/sync` ingests after the seed. *Close:* each observed **selecting a real bill in a real tick and recorded** — the cron payload already carries `countsWritten` and `byTrigger["never-checked"]`, so this closes by reading a `cron_runs` row, not by building an instrument. **HO 789 (2026-10-22), two `/api/cron/bill-rosters` rows from HO 788's FF read:** #26890 (Oct 21, 12:40 PM MT; 18:40:03Z to 18:40:23Z, 20.1 s) recorded `error`, *The operation was aborted due to timeout*, and its payload (`{"ok":false,"elapsedMs":20065,…}`) carries no stats, so it doesn't say what, if anything, the run wrote. The next tick, #26924 (3:40 PM MT; 21:40:03Z, 74.7 s), read `success`. It selected 120 (15 count-ahead, 105 sweep) and fetched 120 in 240 requests. 26 bills changed: 785 cosponsor rows and 169 related rows written, 2 related rows deleted. All 120 were stamped and none deferred, with no errors. Of the 15 rows from Oct 21 00:40Z to Oct 22 18:40Z, every one but #26890 read `success`. One of them, #26986 (Oct 22 03:40Z, 241.2 s), hit its deadline at 97 of 120 with no errors. Read in code, not measured: a Congress.gov failure is caught per bill and deferred (`lib/bill-rosters-refresh.ts:372-402`), and 20,065 ms fits `lib/db.ts`'s 10 s per-request timeout plus its one retry.
- **The roster backfill's resume cannot mark an empty roster as done (opened HO 674).** *Owner:* Code. `scripts/backfill-bill-rosters.ts --resume` reads coverage as **row presence** (`SELECT DISTINCT bill_id FROM …`), so a bill with a genuinely empty roster is indistinguishable from one never processed. Measured on the STEP 2 slice: a resumed run over the same 50 bills skipped 45 and re-fetched **5 — exactly the empty-roster ones** — spending 5 requests against the 80 a cold run spends (that 80 is the control proving the counter moves). **At corpus scale that is a ~3,800-request tax on every resumed run, ~16% of the ~24,400-request job.** Consequence, stated plainly because it changes how the script should be used: **the full backfill should run to completion in one pass**, and a resume after a mid-run failure will re-fetch every empty roster it already knows about. Not fixed at HO 674 — the run completed, so the cost was never paid. *Close:* coverage becomes a watermark rather than a row count — a per-bill "rosters fetched at" stamp, or a sentinel row — such that a resumed run over an already-complete corpus spends **0** requests, demonstrated against a control run that spends non-zero.
- **Nine `if (await X.count())` guards in the e2e suite are latent green-on-deletion tests (opened HO 673).** *Owner:* Code. HO 673 deleted `.v2f-sc-card` and found the test **named for it** — "sponsor hover card renders inside the expand panel" — guarding its only assertion with `if (await card.count())`. With the card gone the count is 0, the assertion never runs, and the test **passes while annotating itself "not a finding."** That instance is fixed (replaced, and proven able to fail: red on pre-change markup, green after). **The pattern is not.** `grep -rn "if (await .*\.count())" e2e/` returns **11** hits, 2 of them the ones HO 673 replaced, leaving **9** (control: 48 total `.count()` occurrences in `e2e/`, so the guard grep discriminates rather than matching everything). Of the 9, **at least 4 wrap an `expect.soft` directly** — `fit-finish.spec.ts:449` (Acted chip rebases `?disposition=acted`), `:473` (agency facet rebases `?agency=`), `:495` (issue bar rebases `?issue=`), `:550` (trade member link points at a member hub) — so if any of those elements is renamed, removed, or broken, the assertion silently does not run and the suite stays green. The remainder (`:479`, `:504`, `:508`, `:633`, `:671`) guard setup clicks rather than assertions, which is the milder form of the same shape. **This is § Gates' subject in its purest form** — the guard makes "feature deleted" and "feature working" produce the same output — and it is the reason that section's reopening criterion exists. *Close (must be able to fail):* every remaining guard either (a) becomes an unconditional assertion, or (b) keeps its guard **and** gains a companion assertion that the guarded thing exists, so absence fails; demonstrated by deleting one guarded element in a scratch build and showing the suite go red where it previously stayed green. A close that reports "reviewed all 9" without that demonstration satisfies neither half.
- **Two `SponsorPhoto` implementations with different image provenance, and a comment asserting there is only one (opened HO 673).** *Owner:* Code. `components/SponsorPhoto.tsx:43` **constructs** `https://bioguide.congress.gov/bioguide/photo/{X}/{BIOGUIDE}.jpg` from the bioguide id; the local `SponsorPhoto` at `components/BillExpandPanel.tsx:152` renders the **stored** `members.depiction_url`, which is a `https://www.congress.gov/img/member/…_200.jpg` URL — **different host, different path, different failure surface**, and each carries its own `onError`→initials fallback. Consumers split accordingly: `AbsenceWatchCards.tsx` and `SponsorExpandedPanel.tsx` take the shared one, `BillExpandPanel` its own. **HO 673 edited the BillExpandPanel copy** (portrait always-on) and deliberately did not unify them — switching the panel's image source is a behaviour change beyond that HO's ruling, and it would swap a stored URL for a constructed one on seven routes at once. The comment at `SponsorPhoto.tsx:25-28` claimed the whole point of its `height` prop was that *"there stays exactly ONE implementation of the bioguide URL and of the `onError->initials` fallback"*; that was false when written, and **HO 673 corrected it in place** rather than only filing it, because a comment asserting an invariant that does not hold is worse than no comment — the next reader reasons from it. *Close (must be able to fail):* one implementation of the URL derivation and one of the fallback, with **both** hosts still reachable — i.e. the surviving implementation renders the same image for a member whose stored `depiction_url` and constructed bioguide URL differ, verified on a named member rather than asserted; and a grep for the retired implementation returns 0 render usages against a non-zero control.
- **The summarize write pair is not atomic, and its failure path skips the flush it needed (opened HO 671 — a pre-existing exposure this HO neither creates nor widens; ruled its own HO on the review).** *Owner:* Code. In the advance branch of `lib/summarize-runner.ts`, `UPDATE bills` (`:315`) and `INSERT INTO stage_transitions` (`:341`) are two separate `db.execute` calls with **nothing wrapping them in a transaction**, and both sit **outside** `processBill`'s `try/catch` (`try` `:234`, `catch` `:255`). So if `:341` throws, `:315` has already committed, `stats.ok++` (`:401`) never runs, the worker's `await processBill(...)` (`:426`) rejects, `Promise.all` rejects, `runSummarize` throws, and **`revalidateTag("bills")` is never reached** — a summary written and never flushed, stale until the 3600s TTL. **HO 671's guard does not change this**: today the throw skips the flush by escaping the route, and after the guard it would skip it by escaping before `ok` is incremented — same outcome, same window, one line earlier. It is recorded here because the alternative was letting a live guarantee the code does not make ("no surface can serve a written-but-unflushed summary") land in the docs — the class of error the HO 670 SKILL guard caught. **The same shape exists in `lib/sync.ts`** (`upsertBill`'s upsert at `:238` then the conditional transition insert at `:272`) with the opposite consequence, and that asymmetry is what kept `/api/sync`'s flush unconditional at HO 671: there the throw is *caught*, so the tick continues and the unconditional flush still fires. *Close:* any one of three, and which one is the HO's call — (a) the pair becomes atomic (a batch/transaction, so a failed transition insert rolls the summary write back), (b) the flush moves somewhere a partial write can still reach it (a `finally`, or a wrote-anything flag set before the second write), or (c) a measurement shows the throw path cannot occur in practice — for which the instrument is `cron_runs` error rows for `/api/cron/summarize` carrying a `stage_transitions` failure, against the 0 such rows in the last 300 ticks. **Its own HO — do not fold it into a feature handoff.**






- **`/welcome` tape unification with `components/MarketsTape` (opened HO 670).** *Owner:* Code, low. HO 670 killed the **config** divergence HO 669 put on the record — `TAPE_SLOTS` and `TapeItem` are gone and every label/format/cadence now comes from `lib/markets` — but two renderers still draw the same data. **The handoff's premise for how to do that was false and is worth recording, because the next person will reach for the same import:** `MARKETS_TAPE`, `ODDS_TAPE` and `ODDS_PAIRS` are **not** exported from `lib/markets.ts`; they are module-private consts in `components/DashboardV2Header.tsx:28`, `:46`, `:56`. HO 670 derived the rosters from the config that IS exported — MARKETS = every `source` `fmp`/`fred` symbol, **verified set-identical** to `MARKETS_TAPE` (`scripts/diagnostic/roster-check-670.ts`, order differs only in WTI's position); ODDS = every `source: "kalshi"` symbol. **What is still duplicated:** render order, the four K↔P pairings (written out rather than inferred from `polyKind`/`polyMonth`, which are two independent fields), and the whole presentational renderer. *Close:* mount the shared component — which would also hand `/welcome` the hover portal and the polled freshness it does not have — **or** export the rosters from a module both surfaces can read so at least the pairing has one home, **or** record why a landing page keeps a presentational copy.

- **The two `/welcome` panel datasets that are not the query the spec named, and the one query that does not exist (opened HO 670).** *Owner:* Code, low. **No dataset was cut for cost:** the rebuilt page measured **94,240 `rows_read`** against the old page's **57,669** — 1.63× under a 3× ceiling (`scripts/diagnostic/welcome-read-budget-670.ts`), so every one of the nine shipped. Three substitutions were forced by DATA, not by budget, and each is named on the surface rather than papered over. (1) **Enacted** — `getEnactedThisWeek()` costs 100 reads and returns **0 rows** in a week with no enactments, which renders a header over an empty panel; replaced by `getFeedBills({stage:"enacted"}, 12)` at 1,588 reads / 12 rows. (2) **Hearings** — the whole forward calendar is **3 rows** during the August recess (`{days:7}` is 1), and a track shorter than its viewport also breaks the scroll loop; `getRecentMeetings(14)` rides along (1,417 reads / 20 rows) and the panel label reads **SCHEDULED + RECENT**. (3) **Members** — the spec asked for "most active 30d" and **no 30-day member-activity query exists**; `getMembersRanked`'s `volume` sort is this-Congress sponsorship, so the label reads **MOST BILLS SPONSORED**. It is also the single most expensive dataset on the page at **20,995 reads**, which is the number any replacement has to beat. *Close:* a 30-day member-activity query with its own measured cost, **or** record that the this-Congress ranking is what the panel wants; and re-check the enacted/hearings substitutions once Congress is back in session (both may simply become correct again, which is a reading, not a fix).

- **`lib/zone-cycle.ts` is client-only because a hook shares the module with a pure formatter, so server components cannot reuse it (opened HO 670).** *Owner:* Code, low. `ZONES` (`:27`) and `formatInZone` (`:66`) are pure and explicitly documented as "safe on server or client"; `useZoneCycle` (`:87`) imports `useState`/`useEffect` in the same file, which makes the **whole module** client-only — a server component that imports the formatter fails the BUILD with "You're importing a component that needs `useState`". **Verified by hitting it:** HO 670's `/welcome` renders its two `AS OF` stamps and `LAST SYNC` server-side, and had to reproduce a 12-line MT formatter inside `app/welcome/page.tsx` rather than call the identical shared one. That is two copies of one format string, which is the drift class this project files rather than tolerates — and the file's own header says the two stamps agreeing is the point. *Close:* split the pure half into its own module (`lib/zone-format.ts` or similar) with `zone-cycle` re-exporting it, and have `/welcome` call it — **gate:** the served `AS OF` string is byte-identical before and after, and the client clock still cycles. **Or** record why the duplicate stays.

- **TWO `MarketsTape` defaults that no live mount exercises — `showMeta` and `scroll` (opened HO 669 — **found by this HO, not created by it**; the inverse of the `BillRowList` entry below, and the distinction is the point).** *Owner:* Code, low. **One entry, not two, because it is one class:** a prop whose default selects a path, where both live mounts pass the other value, so the defaulted path has zero mounts. That is the same test HO 669 applied to the bare branch it stripped — which makes these its residue, found by applying the standard to the neighbours. `MarketsTape`'s `showMeta` defaults to **`true`** (`components/MarketsTape.tsx:27` post-strip) and **neither live mount passes the prop** — `DashboardV2Header.tsx:146` and `:147–155` pass `symbols`/`pairs`/`kind`/`scroll`/`reverse`/`label` and nothing else — so the client's false-arms at `components/MarketsTapeClient.tsx:884` and `:958` (plus the `:638` hooks-must-not-be-conditional comment that exists to serve them) cannot be reached from anything that renders. Unlike HO 664's `BillRowList` filing, **nothing this HO did made that true**: the last `showMeta={false}` caller was the top tape of a two-tape shape that predates the B2 rework; the prop and its default are exactly as HO 669 found them, and were left exactly as found. **Deliberately NOT chased in HO 669**, per the HO 507 shared-component rule: `MarketsTapeClient` is the live client island on `/` and runs past 1,000 lines, so touching it is its own change behind its own gates — and the same two live tapes are the only surfaces to gate on.

  **Instance 2 — `scroll` defaults `false`, and both live mounts pass it, so the STATIC shape has zero mounts.** `DashboardV2Header.tsx:146` passes bare `scroll`; `:147–155` passes `scroll` too. This surfaced out of HO 669's `:2000` SKILL cut-back and is the CSS shadow of the branch that HO 669 removed from the component: the static tape died as a *mount* at HO 608–610 and as a *code branch* at HO 669, and this is the third place it persists.

  **The dead-value caveat, stated up front so nobody reaches for the wrong instrument. What is dead here is VALUES, not SELECTORS, and it is exactly two declarations** — measured, not inferred: (1) `.markets-tape-item`'s `padding: 0 10px` (`app/globals.css:6663`), overridden to `0 11px` by `:6678–6680`; (2) the `width: 28px` on `.markets-tape-meta::before` (`:6956`) and `.markets-tape-label::after` (`:7006`), overridden to `72px` by `:6970–6972`. **Everything else in those base rules is fully live on the scrolling tape** — `display:inline-flex`, `gap:6px`, `white-space:nowrap`, `position:relative`, and both fades' `content`/`position`/`gradient`/`pointer-events` — because the unscoped rules still match a `--scroll` tape for every property the scoped ones do not override. **And `.markets-tape--scroll .markets-tape-item::after` (`:6691`, the HO 363 pipe) has NO unscoped counterpart at all** — one occurrence in the file — so it is purely additive, fully effective on both live mounts, and contributes **no** dead value; it does not belong in this set despite sitting in the same scoped cluster. **Consequence: the HO 668 `.pdc*` excision instrument does NOT transfer.** No selector loses its last consumer, so a built-CSS "selector reads 0" gate would read **green here and prove nothing** — the exact shape of a close criterion whose instrument cannot fail, which this file's own header warns against. Any close needs a per-declaration argument, not a per-selector census.

  **Recorded because it decides how much weight the comment carries: `app/globals.css:6674–6675` already states why the scoping stays, and its stated reason is API reachability, not a live surface** — "*its one caller, the pre-swap classic dashboard, was removed at HO 608 — the scoping stays because `scroll` is still an opt-in prop on MarketsTape*". That is the **weaker** of the two reasons it could have given: it does not claim anything renders unscoped, only that the component's signature still permits it. HO 669 verified that clause is still true and cut its `:2000` SKILL edit back to the verified half on the strength of it. **A keep reason that rests on an opt-in prop is exactly as durable as the prop** — if `scroll` ever becomes required or is removed, this comment's justification goes with it, and the values above become unreachable by construction rather than merely uncalled.

  *Close:* **both instances together, or each with its own recorded reason, and the two have different shapes so do not assume one disposition covers them.** For `showMeta`: strip the branch behind gates on the two live tapes, or record a keep reason. For `scroll`: decide whether the static shape is still wanted as an API affordance — if yes, the `:6674–6675` comment is the keep reason and should say so in those terms rather than by pointing at a caller that has been gone since HO 608; if no, `scroll` stops being a prop, the `--scroll` scoping collapses into the base rules, and the two dead declarations fold away with it. **Do not open this as a CSS-deletion chore** — per the caveat above, there is no dead selector to excise.
*Next builds, roughly ordered. Pull from here when picking up work.*



- **`isSettled` freezes a contest the instant it is SCORED, so a winner that arrives late can never land — the parent of the 11 (opened HO 641, measured HO 640).** *Owner:* Code. `lib/primaries-sync.ts:551-565`: a row is settled ⇔ `primary_date < today` **AND** some `primary_candidates` row carries a non-NULL `vote_pct`. **There is no winner term.** The guard runs at all three sync write paths (`:646` `scrapeSenateSpecialState`, `:905` `syncSenateCandidates`, `:1311` `syncHouseDistricts`) **before the incoming roster is built**, and `status` and `vote_pct` are written in the **same INSERT** (`:685` / `:948` / `:1380`). So a contest scraped in the window *after* Ballotpedia posts results but *before* it marks a winner takes shares + `running` and **becomes settled in that same instant** — every later tick refuses it. It is a race against Ballotpedia's call. **516 of 718 completed contests are settled, and most of those froze CORRECTLY**, having been marked before they settled: the damage is the 11 that froze mid-write, so **the mechanism is the defect, not the count**. The arithmetic closes exactly — 506 with-winner + 11 = 517 against 516 with-shares, the one-row difference being **`senate-MI-2026-R`** (1 candidate, winner marked, no share recorded — uncontested). **TWO dispositions, independent of each other.** **(1) STOP NEW LOSSES — the guard fix, and it is NOT a one-liner.** Adding a winner term to `isSettled` un-freezes every contest that legitimately has no winner — uncontested seats like `senate-ME-2026-R`, where Ballotpedia marks no votebox winner because there is no contest — and those would re-scrape forever. A correct fix needs a **bounded re-check** (eligible for N days past `primary_date`, then settle regardless) or an attempt counter, and **N has to be measured against how long Ballotpedia actually takes to call.** Probe, then build, under its own number. **(2) RECOVER THE EXISTING 11 — the script already ships; do not build one.** `isSettled` is module-private and guards only the three sync paths: **`npm run backfill:primary-results`** bypasses it entirely (`scripts/backfill-primary-results.ts` UPDATEs `vote_pct` AND `status`, no settled check — its only date clause *targets* past contests), as does `reingest:primary-slate`. **Gated on one measurement: has Ballotpedia marked those voteboxes since?** One live fetch against one contest answers it — a live fetch, therefore not a docs-HO action. **Framing, corrected in review:** the freeze is **unreachable by any automatic path, NOT irreversible**. Calling it permanent would justify building a repair that already exists. What is true is the reporting: the cron reports success every tick while never touching these rows — the same-as-success shape (HO 503/506/637) on the ingestion side. **HO 656 — DISPOSITION (2) EXECUTED AND PROVEN ON REAL DATA; DISPOSITION (1) NOW HAS A RECOMMENDED SHAPE AND AN HONEST NON-MEASUREMENT.** The gate was one live fetch of `house-NJ-09-2026-R`: **Pino now parses `isWinner=true` at 51.3%**, so Ballotpedia had marked it since — the freeze is **unreachable, not irreversible**, exactly as the corrected framing said. `npm run backfill:primary-results` then ran off-tick (**398 seats, 370 ok / 28 empty-or-failed, 1,926 rows updated, `vote_pct` 1,663 → 2,077 of 2,527**) and took the frozen set **11 → 6**: **7 recovered** (CA-04/14/16/43, NJ-09, WA-07, WA-09), **4 residual** (WA-05, WA-08, ME-D, ME-R). **The residuals were live-checked, not inferred:** all three pages parse `ok` with **zero winners marked**, so the residue is **upstream-unmarked, NOT a parse failure** — a distinction that changes the disposition, since a parse failure would be our bug. **THE POPULATION IS A MOVING SET, NOT A FIXED 11**, and that is the durable correction: two rows entered the class *during the run* (`house-AZ-03-2026-R`, 1 candidate/uncontested; `house-VA-10-2026-R`, 2 candidates, Aug 4) because writing shares ahead of the call is what settles a row. So the mechanism is **live and continuously recoverable**, and re-running the backfill is maintenance, not a one-off repair. **N CANNOT BE MEASURED FROM OUR DATA — say it plainly rather than fitting one:** we know only that Ballotpedia *had* marked the recovered 7 by the run date, never *when*, so those are upper bounds only; the live residuals give the other end at **9 days (WA) and 65 days (ME) still unmarked**. **RECOMMENDED SHAPE: a bounded re-check window, N = 30 days past `primary_date`, then settle regardless** — chosen **generously, not fitted**; cost is N wasted fetches on an uncontested seat, and at 30 the recent contests stay eligible while ME settles. The attempt-counter alternative needs a column, hence a migration, hence out of scope by construction. **Corpus figures re-derived: completed contests 768** (entry: 718), **with-shares 517 → 658** across the run. **The build is QUEUED below under its own number; `isSettled` was NOT edited here.** **HO 661 — DISPOSITION (1) IS NOW BUILT, and it corrected this entry's own cost model on the way.** The predicate is **past-dated AND (`status='winner'` OR older than `today − 30d`)**; the `vote_pct` term is gone, so the guard no longer freezes the scored-but-uncalled rows this entry was opened about, and the naive-winner-term failure mode it warned of (uncontested seats re-scraping forever) is bounded by the window rather than by a share. **The "N wasted fetches" framing in this entry is FALSE:** `isSettled` is a **write-freeze evaluated after the fetch**, and the cursor selects units with no settled term, so the window spends no fetches at all — it spends N days of rewrite-eligibility, which is the healing. **Disposition (2) is unchanged and still load-bearing:** `backfill:primary-results` / `reingest:primary-slate` remain the recovery past N, and re-running them stays **maintenance, not repair**, because **the population was always a moving set** — a row joins the class the instant it takes shares ahead of its call, which is exactly what the window now gives 30 days to resolve. Live at ship: 4 rows unfrozen, 2 windows running (AZ-03-R to 08-20; the three Aug-4 rows to 09-03).

- **A name market that resolves to no active roster member is a stale-roster detector — already computed, already discarded (opened HO 638).** *Owner:* Code. `favoredMember`'s name-market branch does `roster.find(m => nameKey(m.name) === nameKey(fav.favoriteLabel)) ?? null`: when a market names a **candidate** and no roster row matches, the function has already computed *"this market names someone who is not on this roster"* and **throws it away as a null**, falling back to a party label. On **2026-07-25** that condition went true for `S-ME-2026` on **both** feeds at once — Kalshi (65%) and Polymarket (69%) both naming **Troy Jackson** while the card's roster read Mills and Platner — and stayed true on **every render for roughly two and a half weeks**, on a card whose `last_verified` read a green 2026-07-03. **Scope it precisely: the signal is the NAME-market branch failing to match** (`!favoriteIsParty && favoriteLabel` present, and no roster hit) — **NOT** `favoredMember` returning null generally. An unresolvable **party** market is a normal, first-class state (bare-party is ~65% of matched markets), so gating on the general null would make the detector fire constantly and be muted within a week. Beats `races.last_verified` as an instrument on both axes: it is **derived from an independent feed** rather than curator-set, and it **reads differently on a stale roster** instead of carrying the same-as-success shape (HO 503/506/637).

- **11 contests carry recorded `vote_pct` but no `status='winner'` row — ONE class, not two; the two-class split is FALSIFIED (opened HO 638, rewritten HO 641 on HO 640's measurement).** *Owner:* Code. Surfaced as the third independent bar on the S-ME defect: `backfill:race-challengers` derives general-election challengers from primary **winners**, so a contest with shares but no winner row is inert to it. **HO 639 asserted two classes with opposite fixes — top-two/jungle where a single `status='winner'` row is a category error and the harvest's model is wrong, and ranked-choice as a genuine ingestion gap. HO 640 falsified the first half by measurement.** Across **completed** contest-party `open` contests: **CA 52 total / 48 with winners / 48 carrying exactly TWO winner rows; WA 10 / 3 / 3; and ZERO contests anywhere carry exactly one.** Fifty-one top-two contests are marked correctly with two advancers each — the schema, the ingester and HO 207's two-★ rule all handle top-two properly. The 8 are **not** a model error; **they failed the same way ME and NJ did**, so the 11 are one class: scored, unmarked, and frozen. **The shapes are descriptive, not causal** — 8 contest-party `open` (CA-04/14/16/43, WA-05/07/08/09) · **2** ME ranked-choice (`senate-ME-2026-D` and `senate-ME-2026-R` are separate `primaries` rows; HO 639 counted them as one, which is exactly where its phantom "2 uncharacterized" residue came from) · **1** `house-NJ-09-2026-R`, an ordinary partisan primary — two candidates, Pino 51.5%, a derivable winner, no top-two or RCV excuse. A **fourth shape rides inside ME**: `senate-ME-2026-R` has **one candidate, Collins at 100.0%** — uncontested, so there is no votebox contest to mark, which is precisely why the naive guard fix is wrong (see the freeze parent). **Product impact is 5, not 11:** only NJ-09, WA-05, WA-08, ME-D and ME-R sit on rated seats the harvest reaches; the other 6 are unrated and it never reaches them regardless of status. Measured on the harvest's **real** join — `state` + `chamber` + `CAST(district AS INTEGER)`, gated by `EXISTS(race_ratings for the cycle)` — **not** `primaries.race_id`, which is what HO 640's own first pass used before re-measuring 11 → 5 (oddities). **This line is now a SYMPTOM. Its parent is the `isSettled` freeze, above.** **HO 656 — 7 OF THE 11 RECOVERED, 4 RESIDUAL, AND THE COUNT IS NO LONGER THE POINT.** Recovered: CA-04, CA-14, CA-16, CA-43, NJ-09, WA-07, WA-09. Residual: **WA-05, WA-08** (Aug 4, top-two, 12 and 6 candidates) and **ME-D, ME-R** (Jun 9) — every one of them **live-verified as unmarked UPSTREAM** (`status=ok`, `winners=0`), so none is a parse gap. `senate-ME-2026-R` (Collins, uncontested, 100.0%) remains the expected-and-correct residual this entry already carved out. **The line is superseded as a COUNT** — HO 656 observed two fresh members join the class mid-run, so “11” was a snapshot of a moving set, not a fixed backlog. Track the mechanism on the freeze parent; this symptom stays only for its shape analysis (top-two two-winner marking is CORRECT, RCV and uncontested are structurally unmarkable). **HO 661 — the class SELF-HEALS within N going forward:** a contest that takes shares before its call now stays write-eligible for 30 days, so a since-arrived winner lands on the next tick that reaches it instead of never. The figures in this entry remain dated snapshots of a moving set, and the structurally-unmarkable shapes (RCV, uncontested) still never gain a winner — they now settle by expiry rather than by scoring. **HO 686 — the 4 residual are now 2, and the self-heal claim above is CONFIRMED on the pair that could use it.** `WA-05` and `WA-08` **recovered**: both now carry **two** winner rows (WA-05 Baumgartner 47.4% R / Conroy 18.9% D; WA-08 Schrier 53.8% D / Meline 15.9% R), stamped **2026-08-25**, i.e. **10 days before** their 2026-09-04 floors — a since-arrived call landing inside the 30-day window, which is exactly the mechanism HO 661 bought and the first time it has been observed working end-to-end. **`ME-D` and `ME-R` remain and are now long expired** (2026-06-09, settled by expiry since early July, ~86 days past floor at this reading) — unchanged in kind, still the RCV and uncontested shapes this entry already carved out as structurally unmarkable, so **nothing is owed on them and the expiry is the designed outcome, not a residue to chase**. Read via `scripts/diagnostic/settle-predicate-661.ts` class 2, which lists exactly `house-AZ-03-2026-R` + the two ME rows.

- **There is no ingestion path for a convention nominee — S-ME was hand-seeded and the next one will be too (opened HO 638).** *Owner:* Code. Every automatic route into `race_candidates` is primary-derived (`backfill:race-challengers` harvests `primary_candidates` winners), and a **ballot-vacancy / convention replacement never appears in a primary result** — Jackson was nominated at a 2026-07-25 party convention *after* the June 9 primary had already resolved to a different candidate who then withdrew. So the only way a `nominee` row exists today is a hand edit to `races-seed.json`, and the defect it fixes is invisible until someone eyeballs the card. **Size the demand before building:** how often a 2026 general-election candidate arrives by convention rather than primary is unmeasured. **Note the coupling to the vocabulary decision (HO 638):** `nominee` is a true claim whether or not anything ever writes it automatically, so this line can close as *"permanently hand-curated"* without disturbing the status value — what it must not do is quietly become an argument for collapsing `nominee` back into `won_primary`. **HO 741 named the class and gave it a reader:** a nominee replaced after the primary is the same divergence as an advancer who withdraws — the general-election votebox records both and nothing reads it. the general-box reader is this line's path too, and S-ME is one of its falsification cases. **HO 760 (2026-09-30): the hand-seeded route is gone, and the ballot is the path.** Since HO 760, `seed:races` writes no `nominee` entry (only `withdrew`), and the harvest retires any curated row that is not `withdrew`. So *S-ME was hand-seeded and the next one will be too* no longer holds, and *permanently hand-curated* is no longer a close this line can take. S-ME's nominee now arrives from the ballot: *Troy Dale Jackson*, `nominee`, on HO 760's leg 2. HO 750's ballot path writes `nominee` for a major printed on the box without a primary mark. What remains is a convention nominee in a race with no `box` read, which has no route at all. Still open.






- **M3 re-measure — the build trigger for the queued name-market stale-roster detector (opened HO 643).** *Owner:* Code. Pairs with the detector line above (opened HO 638). HO 642 P1 measured the detector's live population at **88 of 186 rated seats** — a card element naming a person while the roster names nobody. **An alarm firing on 47% of the rated set reads the harvest's staleness, not the defect the detector was conceived for**, and calibrating a threshold against a stale harvest bakes that staleness into the baseline permanently. **Do not build it against this number.** *Promote when:* M3 is re-measured after the harvest-currency line above is closed, and **collapses to the ME-shape handful**. If it stays large, the finding is the harvest, not a missing detector. **DENOMINATOR MOVED 2026-09-21 (HO 743).** The rated set this line's exhibit count is read against went **191 → 118** when the ratings sync began retiring seats the raters call Solid/Safe (House 156 → 83); the harvest's own `ratedIndex` read **190** at its 21:28 run on 2026-09-20 and will read 118 from its next. **A re-measure taken after this date is not comparable to HO 663's 8-of-187 without saying so** — the seats that left were the least competitive in the set, so the exhibits that survive are drawn from a denominator with a different shape, not merely a smaller one.

  > **Restated (HO 659).** The harvest-currency parent closed at HO 656 and the harvest has run since (2026-08-13), so the 642-era figures this entry carried — 88/186 exhibits; 129 zero-roster, 62 derivable, 67 not — are **superseded**: post-run zero-roster is 36, bucketed and summing (28 future primary · 2 incumbent-only · 2 `isSettled` freeze · 3 empty-roster · 1 too-recent). **Trigger:** after HO 660's first *scheduled* fire, re-measure M3 — against a cron-maintained harvest, not a decaying one-off. **THE TRIGGER FIRED 2026-08-14 (confirmed HO 662: `lastRunAt` `2026-08-14T12:30:49.466Z`, `success`, on the `30 12 * * *` schedule), so the re-measure is DUE, not pending.** **Read the number through the buckets first:** the 656 classes are expected residue, not defect; split the exhibits by bucket before calling the shape. **Build the detector only if the remainder beyond the buckets holds the ME shape (a handful).** If it stays large past that discount, the finding is the data, not a missing detector. Calibrating against a stale harvest bakes staleness into the baseline (642 R3) — which is why the trigger waits for the maintained regime.

  > **RE-MEASURED HO 663 (2026-08-14, HEAD `f83f439`) — NO-GO on the detector, and the reason is that the ME-shape remainder is EMPTY, not merely small.** Measured against the maintained regime the trigger required: newest sentinel stamp **`2026-08-14T12:30:50.466Z`**, one distinct stamp across **206 rows / 147 seats**, reconciled to the `cron_runs` row `started_at` `2026-08-14T12:30:49.466Z` `success` 2003ms — the `30 12 * * *` scheduled fire, read from `cron_runs` directly rather than inherited. **Rated set 187 · zero-roster 36 · M3 exhibits 8** (down from 88 of 186 at HO 642 — the figure this entry was opened to prevent building against). **The 8 bucket and SUM, no unnamed remainder:** **1** future primary (`S-AK-2026`, votes 2026-08-18, 16-candidate open field, no winner — a name market ahead of a primary is normal) · **2** winners exist but all are the incumbent, correctly excluded (`NC-07-2026` Rouzer, `S-SD-2026` Rounds — the 656 instances, unchanged) · **1** past-dated, fully scored, uncalled upstream (`WA-05-2026`, voted 2026-08-04, 12/12 scored, 0 winners) · **3** past-dated with an EMPTY roster (`ME-02-2026`, `NY-02-2026`, `NY-22-2026` — `cands=0` on both party rows; HO 564 territory) · **1** too recent to be called (`WI-06-2026`, voted 2026-08-11, three days before the run) · **0** ME-shape remainder. **1+2+1+3+1+0 = 8.** *Promote-when* is therefore **not met and cannot be met by this population**: the entry's own rule builds only if the remainder beyond the buckets holds the ME shape, and the remainder is zero — every exhibit is ordinary pipeline state, so a detector calibrated here would fire exclusively on contests that have not voted, incumbents who won their own primaries, and rosters awaiting an upstream call. **This entry stays OPEN as a WATCH, not closed:** the population is a moving set (28 of the 36 zero-roster seats are future-dated, 21 of them on 2026-08-18 — 18 FL House seats plus `S-AK`/`S-FL`/`S-WY`), so an ME-shape remainder can appear as those resolve. **(a) RE-ARM PREDICTS NO ZERO READ, and nothing above should be read as forecasting the exhibit count decaying to nothing.** Buckets 2 and 4 are **standing populations, not transients**. An incumbent who wins their own primary stays correctly excluded by the harvest for the remainder of the cycle, so **bucket 2 persists by construction**; the past-dated empty rosters are tracked in **the HO 586 M5 entry above** (77 of 560 past-dated house rows carrying 0 candidates — `ME-02-2026` is named among its 8 both-party-empty district holes, and `NY-02-2026`/`NY-22-2026` are the same population), the **HO 564 class**, and that debt is **not drained by this re-measure**, so **bucket 4 persists until it is paid**. Bucket 1 does not vanish either — it *converts*, into a filled roster where the contest rosters and resolves, or into bucket 4 / bucket 5 where it does not. **The re-arm question is therefore the SHAPE of the remainder, never the size of the total:** re-run `scripts/diagnostic/roster-coverage-642.ts`, re-bucket, and **read bucket 6 alone**. *Re-arm date:* after the September primaries close — last future-dated zero-roster contest is `S-DE-2026`, 2026-09-15. **(b) A gated detector build INHERITS the bucket-2 spec decision, and the rule is ROSTER-STATE-INDEPENDENT — an empty roster is NOT the trigger condition.** Before alarming, the detector must resolve the market's favorite label against **incumbent + challengers** — the set `resolveKalshiFavoriteParty` takes (`lib/race-colors.ts`), i.e. the seat's full roster — and **never** against the `race_candidates`-derived roster that `favoredMember` (`lib/race-matchup.ts`) resolves within. **The incumbent lives on the RACE ROW** (`races.incumbent_bioguide_id` → `members`), **not in `race_candidates`**, because the harvest excludes an incumbent-winner by design (`pc.bioguide_id <> r.incumbent_bioguide_id`) — so any resolution keyed on `race_candidates` alone cannot see the very person the market named, **at any roster size**. `NC-07-2026` (Rouzer) and `S-SD-2026` (Rounds) are the worked cases and both happen to carry an empty roster, but the carve-out is not conditioned on that: a seat with a fully populated challenger roster whose market names the incumbent is the identical false positive. A detector without it reproduces HO 642's false-positive baseline on a smaller corpus, which is the exact failure this entry was opened to prevent. **(c) Bucket 3 is WINDOW-OPEN, not a freeze** — the HO 656 label *"the `isSettled` freeze class"* no longer describes it and should not be carried forward. Under HO 661's predicate `WA-05-2026` is past-dated with no winner and inside the 30-day window (floor 2026-07-15), so it is **write-ELIGIBLE through 2026-09-03** and will take the call when Ballotpedia marks one; only on **2026-09-03** does it expire into the frozen state. That date is the later of the two HO 661 settle-window WATCH dates — read that WATCH and this bucket as the same row.
- **77 of 560 past-dated house primary rows carry 0 candidates (~14%) — roster data debt on `/primaries` + `/electoral` (HO 586 M5).** *Owner:* Code, P3. Surfaced (not created) by HO 586's chip fix — the district-first branch resolves a House member to their own `house-{ST}-{DD}` row, and 77 of the 560 past-dated house rows have an empty roster. Of the 77, the **19** that currently host a sitting member's chip were run down by M5: **11** are the incumbent's-party primary being genuinely uncontested (honestly no votebox — the district page parsed fine), **8** are both-party-empty district holes (**AR-01, SC-07, AL-02, ME-01, ME-02, NC-02, AL-07, AR-03**) — pre-existing HO 563-class roster residue or dual-uncontested, undetermined which; **the remaining 58 rows host no current member's chip and are uncharacterized as of HO 586.** Invisible on the member chip (date-only render), but the empty roster shows on `/primaries` + `/electoral`; independent of the chip fix. *Instrument:* count of past-dated house rows with zero candidates. *Close:* the zero-candidate past-row count either falls or every remaining row is explained as genuinely uncontested — the 8 holes explained-or-traced AND the 58 uncharacterized at least bucketed (uncontested vs hole); the count instrument must not read 77 while the item reads closed.

The data is correct per HO 420/422 (Kiley genuinely switched R→I 2026-03-09 while caucusing R — not a bug), and HO 586 makes the gap more conspicuous by giving nearly everyone else a working chip. *Close:* a decision — either an intentional-and-documented gate, or an all-party chip for I members.
- **`primary_type` is fallback-defaulted for ~45 states (HO 584).** *Owner:* Code, low (**P3**). `primaryTypeFor` (`lib/primary-calendar-scrape.ts:62-67`) returns `open` for every state outside top_two/top_four/ranked_choice, so `open` is an **unverified catch-all** across ~45 states, not a determination — LA senate's stored `open` is the known-wrong instance (LA senate is `closed` under Act 1/640), and SC's `open` is right only by coincidence (oddities: *a default is not a determination*). *Close:* replace the fallback with a determined per-state map, or a distinct `unknown`/NULL default so a defaulted value is distinguishable from a determined one. *Instrument:* a determined-vs-defaulted count over `primaries.primary_type`.

- **Absence Watch — a front-page strip naming members who have missed a vote threshold (HO 588 STEP 0; filed HO 603).** *Owner:* Code. **State: SHIPPED at HO 622** (`abd8768` data layer · `12b3e57` band), after the HO 621 probe redux re-ran it against a corpus four months newer than the one that scoped it. **THE FORK RESOLVED TO NEITHER ARM — it went to the STREAK, and the ruling is three sentences.** A cumulative trigger makes a **present-tense claim off a career statistic**, and the 621 corpus priced exactly that: **6 sticky false positives to 1 blind false negative** — six House members above 15% cumulative had missed 0–2 of the last 30 roll calls and five of them voted on the final pre-recess roll call, while Biggs (26 of 30 missed, 9.0% cumulative) was invisible to it. **The copy is dated, never "now"** — *no vote cast since {date}* survives a recess, and the House had not voted in 15 days on the day this shipped, so a present-tense phrasing would have been false about that entire chamber the moment it recessed. **The division of labour is acute-vs-chronic**: this band is *acute* (gone), the HO 527 participation dot-plot and the HO 535 MISSED sort are *chronic* (spotty), so a 26-of-30-with-streak-0 member is correctly absent here and correctly present there — do not later "fix" one surface into the other. **WATCH — S = 30 is launch-conservative.** Revisit after a live cycle. A long-empty strip is C4 working, not a broken threshold (the empty state renders nothing at all, by design); the signal that S sits above the corpus is a strip that **never fires across a full ACTIVE session**, which is a different observation from "it is empty today during a recess." **Live at ship (2026-08-07): exactly two rows — McConnell (51 consecutive, no vote cast since Jun 11, 14.1% of 119th) and Wilson (34, since Jul 16, 29.3%)**, the streaks being the true figures past the probe's 30-roll window cap. Cost as shipped: **1,064 rows returned / ~516ms cold**, one regen a day on the `votes` tag. *Superseded below — the fork text is kept because it is the reasoning the ruling overturned:* **THE FORK WAS UNRESOLVED AND WAS RECORDED HERE AS UNRESOLVED:** **R2** = cumulative missed *rate* vs **R3** = a recent window. R2 was preferred in the design conversation as **sticky** (a member cannot fall off the strip by having one good week) and as **near-free**, because it reused `getParticipationStrip` at a marginal read of ~0. **THE COST HALF OF THAT ARGUMENT HAS EXPIRED — this is the reason not to resume from the design conversation.** HO 594/595 rewrote the participation path onto the materialized `member_participation` table, and HO 602 measured that the **pre-materialization `getParticipationStrip` had been 22% of the entire account read budget** (91.3M rows / 30d). *"Reuses `getParticipationStrip`, marginal read ~0"* was a true statement about a function that no longer exists in that form. **Re-derive the cost of BOTH arms against the current materialized path before choosing.** R3's standing merit is unchanged and is what cumulative is blind to: **a member who stopped showing up last month is invisible inside a long denominator.** **Population + delegate carve, copied from `getParticipationStrip` so the strip and the HO 527 dotplot cannot disagree:** `is_current = 1`; `HAVING COUNT(*) >= PARTICIPATION_FLOOR` (50); delegates carved by the territorial predicate `chamber = 'house' AND state IN ('DC','AS','GU','MP','PR','VI')` — **population-correctness, not outlier-trimming** (HO 527: the real high-miss tail is a *voting* member, Wilson at 29%). *Scope:* **re-read `scripts/diagnostic/absence-watch-588.ts` AND ITS OUTPUT before scoping. Do not resume from the design conversation that produced the fork — it predates the materialization.**
- **Dashboard redesign — the two-region declutter (HO 590 mock arc; filed HO 603).** *Owner:* Design + Code. **State: SHIPPED across HO 608–610 (P3).**  Originally: iterated through **revision J** in conversation. **THE MOCK FILE DOES NOT EXIST** — `mock-590-dashboard-declutter.html` is absent from `docs/design/` entirely (not merely untracked; audited HO 603 against both the worktree and `git ls-files`). `docs/design/dashboard-2col.html` is **NOT** it — that is an HO 254 artifact (`f0cec04`, 2026-06-17) with none of this arc's markers. **So this entry IS the artifact; there is no pointer to follow.** Decisions, each of which was *corrected during* the arc and will be re-litigated if not written down: **(1) Two regions** — a full-height bills/movers column on the **RIGHT**, spanning **from the very top, level with the masthead**, NOT starting below the nav; everything else stacks in the left region (masthead, market/odds tapes, nav, absence watch, hearings, week strip, breaking news, distributions). **(2) The tapes stay HORIZONTAL** — they are not converted to a vertical rail. **(3) Iteration on the existing dashboard — NO parallel v3 route**; sequenced handoffs against the live page (the HO 311 `/dashboard-v2` swap is the precedent for why a parallel route accrues a sunset loop). **(4) Row-level packing:** elements pack **left** via flex, because far-right-anchored elements were stretching rows and opening horizontal voids. **(5) Bills-column readability:** no boxed bill IDs, no chip borders, no sponsor underlines; a quiet **two-line** format; colour reserved **EXCLUSIVELY** for stage status (ENACTED green / FLOOR amber / CMTE dim); date-grouped sections **TODAY / YESTERDAY / EARLIER THIS WEEK**. **(6) CUT IN THE ARC — do not re-add:** the **Polarization band** (slow-moving data not worth its space), the **five-column week grid** for hearings (collapsed to a single-day schedule + a week ribbon), and **masthead counts that duplicated the stage-distribution chart**. **SHIPPED ACROSS HO 608–610 (P3).** All six decisions landed against the committed `docs/design/dashboard-layout-target.html`. **Three of them shipped as REFINEMENTS of the decision above, not violations of it, and each is recorded so it is not re-litigated as drift:** **(a) the Polarization band** was CUT as a band (decision 6) but its numbers survive **in miniature** as three chips in the week strip (`.weekly-band-polchip`, HO 608 §5) — the committed mock shows them, and the mock is the later artifact, so "CUT — do not re-add" applies to the full-width band and NOT to the chips; **(b) date-grouped TODAY / YESTERDAY / EARLIER THIS WEEK** (decision 5) was **superseded by the mock**, which has no date groups in the feed — those strings belong to the hearings day bar — and the rows carry a per-row relative age on line 2 instead (HO 609 §0); **(c) the masthead** packs fully left (decision 4) **deviating from the mock**, whose own `.mast` pins SIGN IN right with a `.sp{flex:1}` spacer — that spacer is exactly the C1 pattern, and SKILL owns the conventions, so the mock loses this one (HO 610). The **nav one-row-at-≥1200px target is RETRACTED**, and its shape is the reusable part: **a fixed-width target set before the element moved into a narrower container measures the old container.** The nav moved into `.dash-left` at HO 608, the feed column takes ~432px of the width the target was computed against, and no amount of nav tuning could meet a number derived from the old geometry. If the nav's wrapping is worth fixing it needs a new target measured against `.dash-left`, not the retracted one. **TWO MORE MOCK DEVIATIONS, recorded at HO 623 so the mock's numbers stop being the record where the app has moved past them.** **(d) The feed column is `minmax(480px, 31%)`, not the mock's 400/27%** — an owner ruling; measured, the column reads **779px at 2560 and 480px at its 1440 floor** (from 678 / 400), both under the ~850px C8 ceiling past which a row of text buys nothing from more width. **(e) The `bxp` container query is `max-width: 520px`, not 440** — and the reason is the reusable part, because *the handoff's stated reason for it did not survive measurement*: at 1440 the panel's inner width is **420px**, already below the old 440, so 1440 would have stayed stacked either way. The boundary that actually moves is **1680**, where the inner width is **446px** — under 440 the widened column would have flipped it to wide mode at a cramped 446px. **A container-query threshold is derived from the container it was tuned against, so widening the container obliges you to re-derive the threshold or it silently changes which viewports collapse** — that is the transferable rule, not the two numbers. `/bills` was checked rather than assumed (its panel is 1140px at 1440, far past either threshold), which is the point of a container query over a route branch.
- **Layout audit — the C1–C8 conventions and their M1–M6 instrument (HO 591 origin; filed HO 603; C2 SHIPPED HO 604; audit BUILT AND RUN HO 606).** *Owner:* Code. **State: BUILT AND RUN.** **This entry now reads M1–M6, not M1–M7, and the count change is deliberate: M7 was the C2 blast-radius static measurement, and HO 604 discharged it PRE-BUILD** — its numbers live in the globals.css --fs comment block and arc-md §2, so the audit implements six. `scripts/diagnostic/layout-audit-606.ts` (`e794194`, corrected `94f6543`) is on main and its numbers are in the HO 606 pasted output. C2 shipped at HO 604 (identity `4185325`, ladder `fd069b9`, rider `5b9c34c`). **Still unbuilt: the C1/C3/C4/C7 remediation — P3 onward.** **Read `docs/design/layout-conventions-arc.md` §3 first — it holds the full spec; this entry holds only what must survive independently.** **THREE OF THE EIGHT CONVENTIONS HAVE NO AUDIT INSTRUMENT, BY DESIGN — a decision, not a gap:** C5 and C6 are **review rules** (enforced by a reader, not a script) and **C8 is C1 seen from the other end** (a too-wide row presents as a C1 gap). So the audit prices four conventions, not eight, and phases are done on M1–M4 reaching zero while C5/C6 ride the eyeball pass. **Scoping findings that cost a session to establish and must not be re-derived:** test against **`next start` (a local production build), NOT `next dev`**; **`/` is gated behind a `ct_seen` cookie** (`app/page.tsx`) that must be set context-wide or the crawl measures `/welcome` and reports a clean dashboard it never visited; **M6 needs no browser at all** and is static analysis; and **900px is a BAD single test width** because it is the breakpoint at which the dashboard grid collapses — bracket it (1024/900/720) rather than measuring only at it. **VERDICT CONDITION — and HO 606 is now the worked example, not HO 604: the detector must be proven to fire on a known defect before any zero is trusted.** Pointed at `/`, the first build of M1 read a **10px** interior gap on the breaking row whose headline visibly ends ~1,100px from its timestamp, and M2 read zero; the leg **refused the crawl**, which is the whole reason it runs first. **WHAT A RE-RUN MUST KNOW:** (a) **exclude or dedupe the three redirect aliases** — `/races` and `/primaries` 308 → `/electoral`, `/members/pass-rate` 307 → `/members?sort=passrate` — the script's route list **still contains them** (`layout-audit-606.ts` ~L95–98) and removing them is a **code change deferred to the next audit run**, not a doc sweep; uncorrected they inflate site-wide M1 from **1,329 to 1,902**, and **27 distinct pages is the real denominator**, not 30. (b) **M1b counts INSTANCES, NOT PROBLEMS** — `/members`' 565 is one row component rendered 565 times, so **remediation is priced per component**, and a phase plan that reads the raw count as 565 units of work is reading it wrong. (c) **Raw counts inflate ~1.4×** because one visual gap is counted once per ancestor wrapper; dedupe to the innermost element of an ancestor chain carrying the same gap. (d) **The per-route counts are the cross-run currency; selector paths are human pointers only** — remediation renames classes, so a cross-run comparison keyed on class names goes blind exactly where the fix landed (the HO 604 keyed-diff oddity). **Where the numbers live:** the pasted HO 606 output, and `docs/handoffs/606-artifacts/audit-2560.json` — local-only, repo-ignored, **regenerable by re-running**, so do not treat it as a historical record. **PHASE PLAN off the table:** P3 is `/` against the committed `dashboard-layout-target.html` (+ the `dashboard-classic` removal, now cheap per the corrected M6); **P4 opens `lobbying` then `members`** — `lobbying` because 78 of its 88 are **M1a** repeated-row anchors, the canonical C1 defect and the cleanest test of whether the fix generalises, and `members` because its 565 M1b wide singletons plus the worst narrow-width surface on the site (724/225/1300 overflowing elements at 1024/900/720) make it the C1/C8 case; **`changes` keeps its P6 slot but LEADS it**, with **`stale` riding alongside** — M3 14.34 and 14.23 against a 2–5 norm, and 42,255px + 25,555px reserved, i.e. **two routes holding two-thirds of the site's 102,450px of reserved space**, which is a C3/C4 problem and not a C1 one at all. **HO 610 — P3 CLOSED, and THE INSTRUMENT IS NOW v2; every number above this paragraph is v1 and they do not compare.** **What changed in v2 (`e6bbc08`), four things, no threshold moved:** vertical-interval **overlap** banding replaces `round(centreY/4px)` bucketing — v1 split any baseline-aligned row whose items differed in size, which under a six-token type ladder is EVERY row, and it scored a packed breaking row at **873px** because it measured id→age with the headline sitting invisibly between them;   **HO 620 — THE ARC IS CLOSED, AND THE INSTRUMENT IS v4. Every number above this paragraph is v1, v2 or v3.** **The era wall now covers M3 and M4 as well as M1/M5** — v4 (`59a45cd`, fixup `62c25e4`) split M4 into **M4-true vs M4f (furniture)**, added **M4w** (width-reserved slots, invisible to a height-keyed emitter by construction), added **M5s** (scroll-by-design, counted once at the outermost of its chain), and made **M3 sample every repeated-sibling group with ≥2 element children** instead of reusing M1a's `kids >= 3` gate. **No threshold moved.** M1/M1b/M1x/M2 are untouched by construction and the bridge run proves it: v3 and v4 read the same DOM (`62c25e4`) and **M1 17 · M1a 0 · M1b 17 · M1x 332 · M2 3 are byte-identical across the two rulers**, while v3's M4 601 / 57,458px **partitions exactly** into v4's M4-true 14 / 853px + M4f 587 / 56,605px.   **THE MAINTENANCE LOOP — four steps, and they are the price of touching layout.** (1) **Run the fixture legs first**; v4 reads **18 of 18 exact** and a zero is not trusted before they are green. (2) **Label the era on every number**, and never present an instrument note as a remediation claim. (3) **Re-check BOTH identities after any change** — site M1 against the residue register, site M1x against the exemption registry — because they are what "done" means here and a drift in either is a missing line, not a rounding. (4) **Any new `data-viz-row` marking ships with an APPLIED curve and both units** (`M1x <candidates> / <rows>`), and the site total is restated.

  **A KNOWN CAVEAT THE LOOP CARRIES, and it is deliberately NOT another instrument revision.** M4's emitter has **three** false-positive kinds, and v4 closes two of them. It catches (a) short labels in repeated rows — that is **M4f**, split off and counted separately — and (b) short *populated* text generally, which HO 617 named when `/member-detail`'s 2,312px turned out to be 27 populated elements rather than a reserved panel (**M4 counts SHORT-TEXT elements, not empty ones**). The third kind survives v4 by construction: **populated non-repeated chrome**. `/members` reads **M4-true 2 / 84px**, and that 84px is the nav strip and the search box — real content, but not inside a repeated-sibling row, so the furniture clause cannot reach it. **Read a small M4-true figure as "check what it is", not as reserved space.** Widening the furniture clause to cover non-repeated chrome would need its own falsification leg and its own fixture pair; it is not worth an instrument revision for 84px, and the caveat is cheaper than the rev.

  **MAINTENANCE ITEMS, FILED WITH THEIR PIXELS AND NOT FIXED** (found by the terminal crawl; none is a defect the arc owed, and each is small enough that a future HO can take it or leave it). **(a) `/`'s document-overflow chain at 720, +19px** — `home-shell` / `home-main` / `dash-left`, three ancestors carrying the same 19px, which is what most of `/`'s residual bucket-(b) 8 at 720 is. **(b) `/`'s races box at 720, 44px of overflow.** Both are narrow-width only; 1024 and 900 read bucket (b) 0. **(c) `/members`' M4-true 84px**, per the caveat above — a nav strip and a search box, not reserved space. None of the three is on the residue register, because the register is M1 and these are M4/M5.
- **THE `data-viz-row` EXEMPTION REGISTRY (opened HO 610, extended HO 615 / 616 / 617 / 619, made standing HO 620).** *Owner:* Code, standing. **This entry exists so the exemption cannot grow silently — an uncounted exemption is how an audit stops measuring the thing it was built for.** **THE CREEP GUARD, verbatim, and it governs every future marking:** *the attribute marks rows containing a bar or shared-axis encoding, never plain text tables, and every new marking ships with its curve.* **Its second clause (HO 719), for the second marking:** *`data-col-flow` marks a container only where CSS multi-column computes and the gap is the column gutter, never a row inside it, and it too ships with its curve.*

  **THE KIND ANNOTATION (added HO 620, from the HO 617 axis pair) — record what the position ENCODES, because that is what decides the verdict.** Clause 1 asks for "a bar or shared-axis encoding", which reads as *bars only* and is too narrow: **a positional encoding qualifies with no bar in it at all.** The worked example is `.ideology-axis` (6) — a fixed −1..+1 DW-NOMINATE domain with positioned markers, where the **distance between two party medians IS the finding** and narrowing the track costs the encoding linearly. **The discriminator is MAGNITUDE vs SEQUENCE, and HO 617 got opposite verdicts out of the same applied method on the same page:** `.ideology-axis` encodes magnitude and was **marked**; `.v2f-bar`, the stage bar, encodes **sequence** — position says *which step*, not *how much* — so compressing costs the reader nothing, a cap reaches it (live 2486 → 379px worst · 1000 → 132 · 640 → 72), and it was **capped with no attribute spent**, scoped to `.bdp` per SKILL's HO 507 shared-rule guard. **File every marking with its kind (magnitude / shared-axis / sequence), and a `sequence` reading is a presumption AGAINST the attribute.** **The metabox outcome, recorded because it is the other direction:** `/bill/[id]`'s three topic chips were argued at HO 619 as exempt-by-context (a metabox *field* is not a repeated row, and C3's noise target is per-row multiplication) — **that argument was true and was not what the rule asks.** The rule asks what the chip *does*; they are labels, and HO 620 (`25493bd`) made them plain. **A context argument is not a kind argument, and only a kind argument reaches this registry.**

  **THE REGISTER — ten marked elements (eight `data-viz-row`, two `data-col-flow`; nine at HO 719, and the heading read "seven" while listing eight until HO 719 corrected it with entry (9)), each with the measurement that justified it:**
  **(1) `StageFunnel`'s `ul` (HO 610).** The dashboard stage funnel. A bar row is label · bar · value-on-a-shared-axis, so the space between a short bar and its number is the encoding. **M1x 13 / 6 visual rows** on `/` (and the same 13 / 6 on `/?stage=committee`, the same component) — the six stage rows plus their `li` wrappers plus the `ul`. Filed in both units retroactively at HO 616, when the caveat below was written.
  **(2) `FirmsLeaderboard`'s table (HO 615, `8a05ad4`) — CURVE CORRECTED HO 616; the exemption stands.** Void: between the firm NAME and **the bar column's fixed origin at x=2107**, 476–699px, median 600, on **25 of 25** rows. That origin is what makes the bars comparable at all.
    **THE HO 615 CURVE WAS MODELLED AND ITS KEY CLAIM WAS FALSE.** It read *"cap 260px clips 2 names and leaves 25 of 25 over threshold; cap 300px clips 0 and leaves 25 over — a cap cannot reach it."* It was computed from track arithmetic — subtract each label's own excess over the cap — which reports *no change* for any cap wider than the longest label. That misses the actual effect: capping a `1fr` label track **moves the fixed 150px bar track left**. **Applied and re-measured (HO 616):** cap 180px → **worst 114px, 0 of 25 over, 10 of 25 names clipped** · cap 220 → 154px, 3 over, 5 clipped · cap 260 → 194px, 7 over, 2 clipped · cap 300 → 234px, 15 over, 0 clipped. **A cap CAN reach it.**
    **The exemption is kept on the honest numbers, not the old ones.** The reachable cap is 180px, and it clips **10 of 25 firm names — 40%**. Against this arc's knees (`/members` 16 of 537 = 3%, `/trades` 4 of 1,209 = 0.33%) that is not an acceptable knee, so clause 2 is satisfied by the corrected curve rather than by the wrong one. Clause 1 was never in question: it is a bar chart on a shared origin.

The modelled curve happened to reach the right answer here; the applied one explains it.
