New sum: 0 + 0 + 125 + 94 + 65 + 4 + 18 = **306**. **The visual-row count is UNCHANGED at 131 across eight markings on six routes, and that asymmetry is the entry** — this is the unit caveat above finally biting in the other direction. Widening the bills column to 45% narrowed the left region until the widest node under `[data-viz-row]` read **518px against the 600px candidate gate**, so the funnel and topic rows stopped being M1 *candidates* at all; the attribute still reaches the same two `ul`s and the same 86 visible nodes beneath them (measured at 45% and at an injected 31% on one build — only the widest moved, 659 → 518). **A marking can therefore be DORMANT: present, correct, and exempting nothing, because nothing under it currently qualifies.** M1x is a candidate count and is thus a function of layout as well as of the marking, which is why leg B no longer asserts it — see the HO 629 roadmap block. **Consequence for the closing identity: site M1x is width-dependent and must be recomputed, never carried forward, whenever a column fraction moves.**
- **THE PERMANENT-RESIDUE REGISTER (opened HO 619, made standing HO 620).** *Owner:* Code, standing. **The twin of the exemption registry above, and the pair of them IS the arc's definition of done: site M1 = named residue + M1x, nothing unexplained.** Where the registry names what left M1 by exemption, this names what **stayed in M1 and was formally accepted** — with its pixels, its class, and **its price**, so that reopening any line is a priced decision rather than a rediscovery. **Site M1 17 at the terminal crawl (`62c25e4`), entry by entry:**
  **(1) `/members` 13** — **11 knee-marginal** `a.mc-row` at **121–131px** (one to eleven pixels over threshold; the HO 612 knee, priced deliberately: the 240px name cap bought 11 rows for 16 truncated names, and buying these 11 costs **16 more truncations**) + **2 `.mc-row-hdr` at 230px** (a short column label over a wide data column — the header-axis class, irreducible without moving a label off the column it labels). Both classes were **probed rather than assumed** before being called accepted (HO 616 `c400450`).
  ~~**(2) `/vote` 2 — the plateau residual** (HO 617 `cd8c3d7`). The applied column-width curve plateaus at 132px and then breaks: (260,24) → 196 · (240,16) → 160 · (228,12) → 132 · (220,12) → 132 · (200,12) → **1192**. Below ~240px the binding term stops being the column width and becomes the ragged fill of the final column. **No width reaches zero**, so this is filed decomposed rather than chased; 0 names wrap at the shipped 240.~~ — **LEFT HO 721: `/vote` re-reads M1 0 · M1c 3; the plateau residual was column flow, and the instrument that could say so arrived at HO 719.** Re-read at `c18a3b4` (local production build, `--route vote`, 2560, hit 2): **M1a 0 · M1b 0 · M1c 3**, both leak lines 0, against HO 721 STEP 0 row 5's pre-state **M1b 2** at 149 / 149px. **Where it went:** exemption-registry entry (10), marking `data-col-flow`, kind flow, with the curve re-applied at three widths and two injected widths. The HO 617 curve stays in this line as history; the line is cited, never subtracted (SKILL, *accepted residue is cited*).
  **(3) `/lobbying` 1 — a column header**, priced at HO 615 and re-affirmed at HO 619. Same class as (1)'s header pair.
  **(4) `/trades` 1 — the 196px amount-column header** (HO 616 `7eba4be`). Entirely the amount column's header-label slack: a 191px track right-aligning "Amount" (52px) leaves 139px, plus the type track's slack on "Type" (45px) and the 12px gap. **Irreducible without either narrowing a column sized to stop the money wrapping, or moving a header label off the column it labels.** The 50 data rows read 114px, under threshold.
  **THE HEADER-AXIS CLASS IS THREE OF THE FOUR ENTRIES** (`/members` 2, `/lobbying` 1, `/trades` 1) and it is the arc's honest floor: a column header must sit over the column it labels, and when the column is sized by its widest datum the label cannot fill it. **Do not re-derive these as new findings.** *Close:* never — this is a register, not a task. **Adding a line requires the same standard as the registry: px, class, price, and the site M1 arithmetic restated.**

  **RESTATED HO 633 (`18f6e68`) — entry (1) `/members` 13 → 9, and site M1 17 → 13.** Measured, not derived: `--route members` against a local production build at this commit, fixture legs green first (18 of 18 exact), read off `m1.rows` rather than the summary line. **Decomposition: 7 knee-marginal `a.mc-row` at 121 / 123 / 123 / 124 / 124 / 126 / 129px + the 2 `.mc-row-hdr` at 230 / 230px.** So **four of the eleven knee rows crossed under threshold, the band narrowed 121–131 → 121–129, and the header pair HELD at 230px, unmoved.** That split is the entry: the knee was always a distribution sitting one to eleven pixels over the line, so a change in text metrics moves it, while the header-axis class is structural and cannot be moved by type at all — which is the register's own claim about why those two entries are different, arriving as a prediction that held. **The intervening change is the HO 633 sans face swap to IBM Plex Sans, and it is named as the CANDIDATE, not as established cause** — no before/after was run on one build to isolate it, and the `/lobbying` WATCH is the standing proof that this crawl reads deltas the font did not cause. **The four crossed rows are NOT a remediation and buy nothing forward**: they were already priced as accepted residue at HO 612, and a metric change that happens to carry them under a threshold can carry them back over on the next face, weight, or cap change. **Site M1 arithmetic restated: 9 (`/members`) + 2 (`/vote`) + 1 (`/lobbying`) + 1 (`/trades`) = 13.** **CITED, NOT SUBTRACTED, per the accepted-residue rule: this 13 excludes the two unattributed `/lobbying` rows held in WATCH (179 / 129px), so the honest reading is 13 with up to 2 unexplained — 15 if they attribute.** The closing identity is therefore **open on one line** until that WATCH resolves; it is the only thing between the register and balancing again.

  **RESTATED HO 634 — the `/lobbying` WATCH closes, the register takes a SECOND `/lobbying` line, and the closing identity BALANCES. Site M1 13-with-up-to-2-unexplained → 15, nothing unexplained.** Disposition **(a) accepted residue**; `(c′)` per-row content sizing was measured and DECLINED; **`.lob-filing-row` ships unchanged at `minmax(0, 340px)`** — no code, no CSS, no component touched.
  **FILED AS ADOPTED-UNVIEWED, and that is part of the record, not a footnote.** The eight paired renders under `docs/handoffs/634-artifacts/shots/` were **never opened**. The ISSUES-column loss is the thing this HALTed for and it was ruled **on the written recommendation, not by eye**. A later reader must know which kind of decision this was: the shots stay on disk as the **unexercised record**.
  **(i) THE HEADER LINE, RESTATED ON THE FOUR-TERM FORM — WITH ITS PRECONDITION IN THE LINE, NOT A FOOTNOTE.** The header-axis gap is **`gap = track_A − ink_A + column_gap + inset_B`**, where `inset_B` is the next cell's ink offset inside its own track (0 when left-aligned, `track_B − ink_B` when right-aligned). **VALID ONLY WHERE `inset_A = 0`** — the label's own ink flush to its own track's left edge; where it is not, the outgoing gap is not four-term and the form OVER-PREDICTS. Live counterexamples, measured: `.mc-r-num` on `/members` (`inset_A` 21 / 20) and AMOUNT on `/trades` (`inset_A` 139) — the form gives **52 where the surface reads 32**. All three header-axis entries close **EXACT, delta 0**, measured at `1e6435a`, fixture legs 18/18 green first: `/members` **209** (240 − 43 + 12 + 0) and **230** (320 − 122 + 12 + 21) · `/trades` **196** (80 − 35 + 12 + 139) · `/lobbying` **215** (340 − 137 + 12 + 0). **`/trades` is the exhibit for why this is not cosmetic: its `inset_B` is 139px and the old three-term form predicts 57 — off by more than the 120px threshold itself.** **Two of the four terms are TEXT METRICS** (`ink_A`, and `ink_B` inside `inset_B`), which corrects HO 633's "cannot be moved by type at all" — see the append-only correction below.
  **(ii) A NEW LINE, `div.mc-row.lob-filing-row` — AND IT IS DELIBERATELY NOT A PX CONSTANT. This is the amendment to the admission standard.** The existing standard (px, class, price, arithmetic restated) assumes a **fixed px** figure, and the fixed-px form is exactly what makes the closing identity checkable arithmetic. **This class cannot have one**, so it is admitted as **class + bound + the statistic behind the bound**: *class* `div.mc-row.lob-filing-row`, data filing rows, NOT header-axis · *bound* **`/lobbying` M1 varies 1–3 per crawl** (header always 1, plus 0–2 data rows) · *statistic* **7 of 81 data rows over threshold pooled across seven filing sets, magnitudes 121–179px**. **The 1–3 bound is measured twice, independently:** the HO 633/634 HALT crawl read per-set M1 3/2/2/3/1/2/1 and the HO 634 close crawl read 1/3/2/2/1/2/1 — same band, different days. *Mechanism:* **`gap ≈ 340 − rendered_ink + 12`**, with the 340 cap fitted at HO 614 to a then-current **minimum** ink of 239; the corpus has since produced registrant→client strings shorter than anything in that sample, and every pixel below 239 goes into the gap. **No commit, no regression — the data crossed under a constant.**
  **(iii) THE PRICE, STATED SO REOPENING IS A PRICED DECISION AND NOT A REDISCOVERY.** `(c′)` — the rc track sized per row and capped at today's 340, `fit-content(340px)` — was **measured, not reasoned**, both modes read in the same page instance against the same live state. It **buys**: over 5 → 1, header gap 215 → 12, and **zero vertical cost** (feed +0px, page +0px, wrapped 63 → 63 — a row wraps iff its natural ink exceeds its track, and that condition is identical under both modes). It **costs**: **ISSUES stops being a column — x-origin spread 0 → 167px at 2560 and 0 → 201px at 1440**, worst at the narrower width because the bound is `340 − min_ink` and min ink shrinks with the type ladder. The header's retirement to 12 is **not a second win**: it retires **because the column it labelled no longer exists**, the same event counted once. And the result is a **partial** column, which is why it was declined by recommendation: **78% of rows (63 of 81) wrap at the bound and hold the old x while 22% (18 of 81) pull left to their own text** — neither a column nor an honest pack. **Residual under `(c′)` is 1 row, and it does not close the class:** that row is the wrap-at-bound case, and its population is **63 rows whose widest rendered line runs 231–338px (spread 107) against the 232px line that decides over-threshold**, on ~24px of margin. **Reopening `(c′)` means re-taking that trade, not rediscovering it.**

- **The padding half of C2 — row padding and fixed-size glyph strips stepping at the type breakpoints (deferred from HO 604, named not dropped).** *Owner:* Code. HO 604 shipped **type only**, deliberately: once the ladder is live you can see which paddings actually need it instead of guessing. **The 2560 eyeball produced its concrete worklist, measured against the pre-arc build at the same width** — each is a fixed-width container that did not scale with the type it holds, not a type bug: (a) **`/lobbying`** — the BY-TOPIC section blurb reflows 1→2 lines (the *only* leaf text element on any crawled route to gain a line), and the TOP FIRMS top-issue label clips harder (20/25 → 23/25) because its column is pinned at `minmax(0,120px)`; (b) **`/`** — the hearings filter bar reflows onto a second row; (c) **`/welcome`** — a feed title goes 1/7 → 2/7 clipped. At **1440 there is no change at all** (0 new wrap, 0 new clipping, 0 computed-size differences vs pre-arc), so this is strictly a wide-viewport item. **The non-scaling set that drives it:** ~68 one-off CSS sizes (8 / 8.5 / 9.5 / 10.5 / 11.5 / 12.5 / 15–38) and **34 SVG `fontSize` presentation attributes**, which are user-unit attributes and **cannot take a CSS var at all** — so a 20px heading sits over 15px body at 2400 instead of over 12px body and the hierarchy compresses at wide widths. That consequence is **recorded in the `globals.css` ladder comment as bounded and real — do not re-file it as a bug.** **Measured nav baseline (HO 606):** `nav.primary-nav.home-header-nav` wraps **1→2 lines at 1024 and 1→3 at 900/720 on every route crawled** — the single most consistent narrow-width finding in the audit. **Nav compaction is P3 scope** (the arc moved it there from the old P1); this is its baseline. *Gate:* run after the audit (HO 606) so padding is fixed against measured rows rather than guessed ones.

Measured by `scripts/diagnostic/skill-coverage-649.ts` on `b634f7e`: of **150** `components/*.tsx`, **12** have **zero** anchored-literal hits in SKILL and are **reachable from an `app/**/page.tsx`** — `AuthButton` · `BillIdChip` · `HearingDetailCard` · `HearingMeetingsEmbed` · `HearingsDaySchedule` · `NominationDispositionBadge` · `RaceHeader` · `RankRow` · `SearchResultsMembers` · `SearchResultsReports` · `SignInButton` · `TopicDistributionList`. **All 30 routes are covered**, so this is component-level only. **The sharpest instance, and the reason this is not bookkeeping:** SKILL's live-client-islands enumeration names **`DashboardTopicTreemap`**, which has zero value-importers, while **`TopicDistributionList`** — the component that REPLACED it, imported and rendered at `app/page.tsx:216` — is absent. The docs describe the dead one and not the live one. **NOT classified here, deliberately:** deciding which of the 12 owes a passage needs criteria written *before* the list is read, and this list has now been read — so the criteria belong to whoever scopes it, stated first. **HO 650 CHANGES WHAT A RE-RUN REPORTS, AND THE DIRECTION IS NOT WHAT IT LOOKS LIKE: 12 → 11.** `TopicDistributionList` leaves the zero-hit set because HO 650 named it in the islands list — but **nothing about it was classified.** It left because a FALSE CLAIM ABOUT A DIFFERENT COMPONENT was corrected and it happened to be the correction's subject; the criteria for what owes a passage still have to be written before the list is read, and the list has now been read three times. **Whoever scopes this must RE-RUN the probe rather than work from the filed list**, and should know that one name left it for a reason that does not generalise to the other eleven. *Close:* each of the 12 is either given a passage or explicitly ruled as not owing one.
- **18 SKILL lines carry a drift-prone figure with no canonical home (HO 649 probe, M2 class 3).** *Owner:* Code. Of **142** dated/HO-tagged lines carrying a measurement, **96 are class 1** (every token also lives in backlog or oddities — priced as: replace with a pointer, the HO 648 treatment) and **46** needed a human read. That residue splits **27 class 2** (the number IS the evidence for the claim it sits in — a historical incident, a measured justification for a design decision — priced as: stays, carries a date, no other action), **18 class 3**, which is the only class that is work, **and 1 instrument artifact — line 2435's `+93.5`**, which is the handoff number HO 93.5 written with a `+` prefix instead of `HO `, so the sub-number filter (which only looks for `HO ` / `handoff ` immediately before a token) did not catch it. **It did NOT turn out to have a home on hand-reading** — the class-1 test never saw it, because the TOKENIZER emitted something that is not a measurement at all; 27 + 18 + 1 = 46 and the first draft of this entry reported 45, having dropped the artifact from the arithmetic silently. Class 3 splits again, and the halves want different handling: **8 are layout constants** (`1715` 56fr/1199px · `1719` 264px · `1720` 430px · `1725` 384/650/210px · `1735` 110/70px · `2026` 126px/0.85 · `2030` 1000/360px · `2338` 430px) whose home is the CSS; **10 are corpus or behaviour figures** (`1050`+`1131` 240s carves · `1124` ~2,700 · `1135` ~468 rows · **`1550` ~16,289 — the clearest single case, a raw corpus size with no home** · `1580` 235 rows · `1581` ~12% · `1587` 60 rows · `1588` ~97 rows · `1589` ~99%). **NOTHING WAS RE-MEASURED** — the finding is which figures are structurally exposed, not which have gone stale; re-measuring turns a bounded classification into an unbounded audit. **THE INSTRUMENT CAUGHT ITS OWN DEFECT CLASS TWICE WHILE BEING BUILT, and that is evidence about the class rather than something to bury.** Both bugs were exactly the too-loose-pattern failure this probe exists to detect, occurring inside the probe: (1) the first reachability graph counted `import type` edges, which are erased at compile time, and reported the known orphan `DashboardTopicTreemap` as live — the direction that reads as a clean bill of health; (2) this M2 extractor matched handoff sub-numbers (`HO 112.2`, `HO 154.1`) as measurements and manufactured **13** false residue lines before the filter was added. **One survives and is named rather than quietly carried: line 2435 above.** A too-loose pattern is not an occasional slip in this codebase — it is the default failure of every identifier- or figure-matching check written here, and a probe designed against it still shipped two. *Close:* each class-3 line gets a home, a date, or a deletion.
- **SKILL documents a deleted ROUTE and a deleted COMPONENT as live, and the probe cannot see either (found HO 650, out of its scope).** *Owner:* Code. While removing the four dead names, more sites turned out to name things that no longer exist. **L1715 is the `/dashboard-classic` entry, a route whose `app/dashboard-classic/page.tsx` is gone** (removed in the HO 608–610 arc). **`DistributionsTabs` — a component with no file in the tree — is named 4x in SKILL**, at L1715, L1716, L2044, **and L2043**. **L2043 IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS, AND IT CHANGES WHAT THIS FINDING MEANS.** That is the line HO 650 CORRECTED (`123e4c3`), and `DistributionsTabs` sits **immediately after** the clause that was rewritten — *“…rendering nowhere), `DistributionsTabs` (**HO 244**, STAGE | TOPIC tab state…”*. So the defect survived **inside a sentence under active repair, in the same commit, read by someone who had just verified two other component names on that exact line** (`DashboardTopicTreemap` and `StateFlag`, both removed from it). **The class is therefore not confined to lines nobody has touched recently — it is invisible to a careful human read, not merely to the probe**, which is a far stronger argument for measuring it than “two stale lines exist” would have been. A name in a list of live components carries no signal that its file is gone: nothing renders differently, nothing fails to compile, and the eye reads it as one more familiar identifier. **AND IT IS NOT A MARKDOWN-ONLY CLASS — `app/globals.css:1563`** carries an HO 244 comment describing the treemap's fixed-height pane and *“only the active pane is mounted (DistributionsTabs)”*: a stale comment about two deleted things, in a file type a docs-oriented check would never open. The third-direction instrument must not assume markdown. **NOT WIDENED AT HO 650, deliberately and on the owner's ruling.** The full documented-and-nonexistent set is UNMEASURED, and editing SKILL against a hand-assembled list is precisely how the L1711-vs-L2044 count contradiction this HO just resolved came to exist. **Enumerate first, in its own HO**, then fix from the enumeration. **THE INSTRUMENT LIMIT IS THE DURABLE PART.** `skill-coverage-649.ts` measures in two directions — *exists and undocumented* (M1) and *documented and unreachable* (the M1 inverse added at HO 649) — and **both start from a file that EXISTS.** A passage describing something already deleted from disk is invisible to both, by construction: there is no file to look up. That is a third direction, *documented-and-nonexistent*, and it is the one that hides the oldest drift, because a file that is gone stops generating any signal at all. Cheap to add: extract the backticked identifiers SKILL claims are live, and check each against the filesystem. *Close:* the third direction is measured, and L1715/L1716 are reconciled against what actually renders.
- **The documented-and-nonexistent set is now MEASURED: 94 phantom mentions over 50 names (HO 651 probe, M1/M2).** *Owner:* Code. `scripts/diagnostic/skill-phantoms-651.ts` on `0ee0570`, answering the HO 650 entry directly above. **M1:** 392 backticked identifiers SKILL names → 127 excluded by a `components/<N>.tsx` file, 67 by an export somewhere in source, leaving **198 candidates with no file and no export**. **M2, per MENTION rather than per name** (a name is legitimately RECORD at one line and PHANTOM at another): 344 mentions → **203 NOT-A-COMPONENT** (vocabulary, 135 names), **47 RECORD** (correctly states the thing was deleted/replaced, 27 names), **94 PHANTOM — presented as live — over 50 names.** **20 of the 50 are corroborated by git history**, on evidence owing nothing to any heuristic in the probe (`PHANTOM_GITCHECK=1` finds a recorded `components/*.tsx` deletion): `BillsIntroTimeline` `ColorKeyStrip` `DashboardLead` `DashboardTopicTreemap` `DistributionsTabs` `DualMarketsTape` `EnactedBanner` `HomeHeader` `MediaAttentionCell` `MemberAffiliations` `OnTheHillBand` `PrimariesMap` `PrimaryDistrictCard` `PrimaryDistrictModal` `PrimaryMapCard` `PrimaryRow` `RacesMap` `ShareBar` `SponsorRow` `TopicDistribution`. **THAT CORE RESTS ON THREE CONVERGENT CHECKS, ARRIVED AT BY DIFFERENT METHODS, WHICH IS WHY IT IS CITABLE WITHOUT RE-RUNNING ANYTHING.** (i) the probe's own exclusion — no `components/<N>.tsx`, no export anywhere under `app/ components/ lib/ scripts/`; (ii) git history — a recorded `components/*.tsx` deletion, which owes nothing to the marker regex, the ownership scope or the vocabulary rules; and (iii) **an independent check by the reviewer against the tree** (no file, no export under `app/ components/ lib/`), done separately from the probe and confirming **18 of the 20**. Re-running that third method over all twenty returns the same answer for every one, so the core is **20/20 under two hand-checkable criteria plus the historical record**. A later reader can cite these twenty from the entry; the other thirty need the re-run. **The other 30 are NOT all real, and the list is deliberately unpruned so that is visible** — the residual is dictionary-word UI labels (`Watching`, `Voted`, `Solid`, `Safe`, `Republican`, `Dem`, `Bills`, `Legislation`) and symbols that lived inside another file rather than owning one (`StageKey`, `TopicKey`, `PrimaryExpandProvider`, `SectionShell`). A list already tidied to look sensible cannot be checked, so the rules that fired are emitted per entry instead. *Close:* every PHANTOM mention is repaired or ruled correct. **(HO 652) `### Pages` IS DISCHARGED, and the counts above are now historical — they remain true of `0ee0570` and are NOT restated here.** A re-run measures **77 PHANTOM mentions, down from 94**, with the `### Pages` section going **47 → 30**. The section was repaired as a unit, every clause grounded in the route's own `page.tsx`, one bullet deleted (`/dashboard-classic`, whose removal IS recorded — `1d9c339`, HO 608, and the roadmap names it twice, so no loop closed unrecorded). **The 30 survivors are NAMED RESIDUE, not unmeasured drift, and 25 of them are ONE classifier limitation worth knowing before 653 touches `### Server / client split`:** a single deletion marker serving a LIST of names — *“`A` / `B` / `C` are retired”* — lands in only the LAST name's ownership scope, so the earlier names read PHANTOM while the sentence is a correct record. The canonical-list section 653 inherits is built almost entirely of such lists, so expect its raw PHANTOM count to overstate its real drift by a similar margin, and price the repair from the mentions rather than the count. The remainder: a paragraph-level historical frame the per-mention scope cannot see (`/primaries`), a locally-defined component the probe's own limits section predicts (`ActivityCaption`), and vocabulary. **And the probe's C1 control expired mid-repair, by a route its own C4 did not predict:** it was anchored to SKILL **line 2043**, and deleting one line 300+ lines above shifted the file so the control silently tested a different mention. **A line-number anchor is invalidated by ANY edit above it, not just by a repair of its subject** — which matters directly to 653, since any `### Server / client split` edit shifts every line below it. C1 is now an inline fixture and no longer expires that way. **(HO 653) `### Server / client split` IS DISCHARGED, by DELETION rather than repair.** A re-run measures **73**, down from 77, with the section going **11 → 10** — reconciled, not asserted: 3 lost at L1711, 3 lost with the deleted name list, 2 gained in the demoted changelog, all other 63 unchanged. **The hardcoded snapshot and its name list are gone**; what remains is one unambiguous refresh command plus the residue a grep cannot produce (the 2 hook files; that one file can carry two client exports — `BillExpandPanel` also exports `BillStageBar`; `MobileNavDrawer` dormant; and the 13 server components that LOOK interactive, each verified present and directive-free). The changelog survives DEMOTED, as history of how the set changed rather than a claim about membership. **THE EVIDENCE THAT THE SNAPSHOT COULD NOT BE MAINTAINED IS NOT THAT IT WAS STALE — IT WAS SELF-CONTRADICTORY.** Measured at `f47b807`: `.tsx`-only **67**, `.ts`+`.tsx` **69**, recursive **69**, none under `svg/`; so the line's own “69 matches incl. the 2 hooks → ~67 components” was arithmetically RIGHT. What was wrong is that the same line closed with “the live count is 55 grep matches = ~53 islands, as of HO 328” — **HO 650 corrected the headline and left the tail, so the `~53`-vs-`~67` disagreement never ended, it moved inside one line.** A figure that reads as authoritative while contradicting itself four hundred words later is a stronger argument for deletion than mere staleness, and it is why the refresh instruction had to become an exact invocation: *“grep under `components/`”* admits readings that differ by exactly the two hooks, so a `.tsx`-only reading followed by the documented hook-subtraction double-subtracts. **A NOTE FOR WHOEVER PRICES THE REMAINING SECTIONS:** the 10 survivors are the list-marker artifact, exactly as predicted before the edit — a demoted changelog is built of *“`A` / `B` / `C` were deleted”* sentences and the marker lands only in the last name's scope. Two of them are names this HO ADDED while demoting. **The count going down by 4 while a section was gutted is the correct reading, not a disappointing one**, and any section repaired by demotion-to-history will behave the same way. **Scope 653 FROM THE ENUMERATION, by re-running the probe — not from this line.** Editing SKILL against a hand-assembled list is exactly how the `~53`-vs-`~67` contradiction HO 650 resolved came to exist, and this list is long enough that a hand transcription of it would repeat that.
- **WATCH — `WeeklyBandMetricCard.prior` went OPTIONAL at HO 654, so a metric that FORGETS it renders no delta instead of failing to compile.** *Owner:* Code. Before 654 the prop was required and omission was a **type error**; now a fifth metric card added without `prior`/`priorDate` silently renders a metric with no week-over-week delta, and **“absence is the claim” is indistinguishable from “someone omitted it” to everything except a human reading the comment.** The prop's meaning moved **out of the type and into a comment**, which is the only place it is now written down. **That is the HO 635 shape arriving inside the fix for HO 635's consequences** — a field whose semantics the type system cannot enforce (`stage_observed_at` was a timestamp column that could not say which clock it was on; `prior?: number` is a prop whose absence cannot say whether it was reasoned about). **It STAYS as-is:** the blast radius is **4 call sites in one file** (`components/WeeklyBand.tsx:189/233/269/292`), all four visible in one screen, so the cost of the looser type is bounded and observable. **The eventual fix shape, named here so the next person adding a metric card doesn't re-derive it: an explicit opt-out prop** — e.g. a required discriminated `delta` (`{prior, priorDate}` | `{none: "no-correct-delta"}`) or a required `noDelta` flag — **so that OMISSION is a compile error again and DECLINING a delta is a positive statement in the type.** *Close:* a fifth metric card lands, or the opt-out prop ships. Do not close by re-requiring `prior`: that would force TRANSITIONS OBSERVED to pass a number it has no correct value for, which is the thing HO 654 declined.
- **A tight string-presence filter is unsound for this class, and the instrument can re-enter the same trap (HO 651).** *Owner:* Code. **165 of the 198 M1 candidates are PAIRED-DRIFT** — the name occurs as a token somewhere in source, so a string-presence check would CLEAR it, while it has no file and no export. The motivating case is the whole argument: **`DistributionsTabs`'s only occurrence anywhere is `app/globals.css:1563`, inside a comment describing the deleted treemap's pane** — one stale artifact vouching for another, and two pieces of drift read as one live component. Hence the probe's rule: **existence means a DEFINITION, never a mention**, with presence reported as a column rather than used as an exclusion. **The same shape re-entered through the instrument itself and was caught by measurement, not by reading:** this probe NAMES components in its own comments and `scripts/` is inside the scanned tree, so before self-exclusion it vouched for a phantom it was enumerating (`DashboardBubbleChart`, vouched solely by `skill-phantoms-651.ts`). **An instrument that discusses its subjects in its own source will vouch for them if its own tree is in scope** — worth carrying to the next probe that scans repo text.**STATE IT AS ONE MECHANISM WITH TWO INSTANCES, NOT AS A PROBE QUIRK:** *an artifact that merely MENTIONS a name can clear a string-presence check for it, and that artifact may be stale prose OR the checking instrument's own source.* Instance one is `app/globals.css:1563` — a comment about a deleted pane vouching for a deleted component. Instance two is `scripts/diagnostic/skill-phantoms-651.ts` naming the very things it hunts, with `scripts/` inside its own scanned tree. **Any future check of this shape inherits both**, and the second is the easier to miss because the instrument looks like the one trustworthy thing in the room. **OWED TO ODDITIES** — flagged here rather than written there, because a probe does not get to add a standing rule; whoever acts on this list should land it. **AND A SECOND WAY THE INSTRUMENT READ AS SUCCESS WHILE MEASURING NOTHING, from the same arc.** HO 651 §4 states the deletion marker sits **four characters** from the L2043 phantom. It does not: **the real distance is ~162 characters**, and the “four” was measured off an ELIDED QUOTE inside the handoff — its own `…` hiding ~145 characters of clause — rather than off SKILL. The consequence was concrete: the control was first built with a ±120-char naive window, which cannot reach a marker at 162, so **it passed under both the correct classifier and the broken one and discriminated nothing.** An inert control that passes is this repo's most-repeated defect, and this is the reasoning-over-reports class arriving *from the reviewer, inside the handoff for the probe built against exactly that*. The fix was to make the control assert the MECHANISM — PHANTOM *and* the adjacent-marker trap demonstrably present — so it now fails under `PHANTOM_NAIVE=1`. **Measure a distance in the artifact, never in a quotation of it.**  *Watch:* `app/globals.css:1563` is the known vouching artifact and is untouched; it is named in the HO 650 entry above as part of that repair, not here. **THIRD INSTANCE, AND THE OWED-TO-ODDITIES DEBT IS NOW PAID (HO 655).** The mechanism is filed at the END of `docs/oddities.md` as *a matching check whose pattern cannot fail for some inputs reports those inputs as passing* — recorded here rather than as a second entry because it is **one mechanism, and splitting it across two entries is exactly what this entry argued against**. The third instance is HO 655's selector census: a **bare substring match** over `components/` + `app/` attributed `.open` to `AbsenceCardBack.tsx` and `.v2f-id` to `BillIdChip.tsx`, **both false** — `open` matched the English word in a comment and `v2f-id` matched inside the longer string `bill-id-chip`. **A short token cannot fail a substring test over a large tree**, so v1's *“0 missing”* was partly a property of the matcher rather than of the specs. **The two polarities are now named:** instances one and two **cannot MISS** (an artifact, or the instrument's own source, supplies the match); the ±120-char window **cannot MATCH** (unreachable evidence, so everything reports absent). **And the result stood while the instrument did not** — the strict re-run (whole-word on a `className=` line, or `.token` in CSS) returned the SAME 0 with correct attributions, so **nothing in the first output distinguished the broken matcher from the sound one.** Agreement is the silent case; disagreement would have announced the defect.
- **WATCH — `fit-finish.spec.ts:466` (`/nominations`) failed ONCE under the full-glob parallel run and passed twice (HO 655).** *Owner:* Code. Passed in isolation and on a second full `npm run test:e2e`; **1 failure in 3 runs, cause unidentified** — most likely contention against a single local server when both specs run together, but that is a guess and is labelled as one. **Recorded rather than dropped** because an intermittent failure nobody wrote down is how a suite stops being run: the next person sees a red they cannot attribute, learns the suite is unreliable, and the NEXT real failure is skimmed. **Not attributable to HO 655** — that HO touched only the feed-id test, and `/nominations` shares no component with it. *Close:* it recurs and is diagnosed, or three consecutive clean glob runs retire it. **SECOND ROUTE, HO 657: `/news loads clean` failed the same way** — red under the full `npm run test:e2e` glob, **passes in isolation** (`console=0 pageErr=0 bad=0`), and shares no component with that HO's change (the dashboard races panel). **Two unrelated routes now exhibit it, which is evidence for the contention guess and against a per-route cause** — the common factor is the parallel glob against a single local server, not the route. The close criterion is unchanged and now has a wider net: diagnose it, or retire it on three consecutive clean glob runs. **Neither HO's change is implicated**, and both were checked in isolation rather than assumed.

## BANKED

*Gated/dated; each states its gate.*

- **Consolidate the two party-split mechanisms (HO 540).** The D/R/I split over a roll call is now computed two ways: a SQL `GROUP BY` in `getBillAmendmentVotes` step 3, and an in-memory reduce on `/vote/[id]`. They **agree by the structural skip-unless-D/R/I guard** (oddities), but it's two implementations of one number. *Gate:* a third consumer — the HO 428-A extraction trigger (extract to a shared helper when the count hits three, not before). *Owner:* Corey.


- **Dead-symbol prune (`market_ticks`)** *(cosmetic, want-not-need)* — the HO 474 recon found ~200 frozen Stooq-era rows (DOW/GOLD/BTC/VIX/DXY/SILVER/NATGAS/ITA/XLK/XLV/XLF/XLE/XLI) inert in `market_ticks` since ~mid-June — not in `MARKET_SYMBOLS`, never rendered. A one-time `DELETE` is pure hygiene, zero functional benefit. *Gate:* only if `market_ticks` size ever matters (it won't at ~74 rows/day).

- **cron-health refinements (HO 477, deferred)** *(monitoring, want-not-need)* — (a) **fmp-specific freshness:** the markets route logs one `route="/api/cron/markets"` for both the bare and fmp crons, so fmp stopping while bare keeps firing isn't row-detectable — needs payload inspection (symbol-count or a `?source=fmp` marker). (b) **window-aware thresholds** for `/api/cron/weekly-report` + `/api/sync-race-ratings` *(HO 771: the ratings route is daily now; weekly-report is the one weekly left)*: the flat 9-day `windowOnly` threshold catches a total stop; precise next-fire alerting needs cron-expression parsing. (c) **per-route data-freshness layer:** a route that times out forever while committing nothing reads alive on `cron_runs` status alone — folding HO 135's bills / summary / news freshness reads into `computeCronHealth` would catch the degrade-not-stop mode. All low-priority; the realistic failures (stopped firing, erroring every tick) are already caught. **HO 741 — a ZERO-ROW SUCCESS is the shape this line exists for, and there is now a live instance:** `/api/sync-race-ratings` returned `scraped 0 upserted 0` under a `success` verdict twice, and nothing anywhere went amber. Any refinement that lands should treat “ran, and produced nothing” as distinct from “ran”. **HO 742 — that instance is CLOSED at the source rather than by a watchdog refinement:** `/api/sync-race-ratings` now throws on a zero, so `cron_runs` reads `error` and `/api/health` reds on `lastStatus`. The general point stands for every other route — a zero-row success is still invisible where the route does not throw — but the route that prompted it fixes itself. **HO 806 (2026-11-07):** (a) is moot: the `?source=fmp` entry is gone, so one cron writes the markets rows. (b) and (c) stand.

- **Nominations secondary surfaces** — (i) a **military-confirmations view** (the 1,049 bulk service PNs excluded from the civilian `/nominations` surface; `is_military` makes it a clean filter/toggle) — this item now **also owns the `nominee_count` hydration**: it's military-only (civilian PNs are 1 nominee) and the natural (only) consumer of officer counts, and the same `--hydrate` detail walk reads it cheaply from the embedded `nominees[]` array (the HO 458 rider), so the military view carries both the `is_military` toggle and the `nominee_count` populate; and (ii) a **`/nomination/[id]` detail page** (rows are non-linked in v1). This caching-pass item remains for warm-path fan-out, not the cold 500.)*
- **`raw_json` rebuild off the `bills` hot table** (HO 241 — migration ABANDONED). `bills.raw_json` is ~27MB inline across 16k rows, bloating the table's physical pages. The split was dropped: `VACUUM` is **blocked on hosted Turso**, so `DROP COLUMN` can't reclaim pages — only a full table-rebuild (lean `CREATE` → `INSERT…SELECT` → swap → recreate indexes/FKs) repacks. **No longer an outage risk** (the cold-start 500 was fixed by indexing, not this). *Gate:* pure storage hygiene — promote only if `bills` table size becomes a real cost, and weigh the no-VACUUM rebuild risk. See `docs/handoffs/241-bills-rawjson-split.md` (banner).
- **Deterministic action-code stage tier ahead of the LLM** (HO 239 banked deeper fix — **confirmed landed in this ledger**). Classify stage from Congress.gov structured action codes deterministically; LLM only for genuinely ambiguous text, never the sole source. *Diagnostic (Jun 12):* 60/230 transitioned bills (26.1%) carried a backward slot pair; 49 were `*→introduced` (impossible). Mechanism: classifier flicker on *stable* input (`119-hr-8467`). Guard caught a fresh one live (`119-hr-8464`) on the first post-fix run — inflow is real and ongoing. *Symmetric-noise inference:* detectable backward hops are only the *visible* half; the same non-determinism produces *forward* flicker inside legitimate-looking transitions (committee→floor when truth is committee→committee), currently **unmeasurable**. So 26% is a *floor* on total misclassification. *Gate:* when stage accuracy starts mattering for a shipped surface (MOVERS hop-count, the funnel, any stage-gated query). See WATCH → guard-rejection telemetry for the priority signal.

The HO 239 **advance-only guard correctly won't touch them** — a downward correction is a regression the guard rejects — so sync never self-fixes them either, and the one-off HO 384 backfill (advance-only too) left them as-is. *Gate:* a deliberate, **pattern-scoped monotonicity-override** handoff that adjudicates only this known `floor→committee` over-store pattern (not a blanket regression unlock). Not urgent — same shape as the ~26-bill topic-tag backfill above. *Instrument:* count `stage='floor'` rows whose `latest_action_text` is calendar/second-reading-only.

- **BANKED constraint — participation *rank* surfaces need a delegate-eligibility carve-out (HO 523/525; dotplot SHIPPED HO 527, sort SHIPPED HO 535).** Both the **`/members` dotplot (HO 527, `7fa729c`)** and the **MISSED sort (HO 535, `b565c13`)** ship WITH the carve-out — the territorial predicate `chamber='house' AND state IN ('DC','AS','GU','MP','PR','VI')` (dotplot drops delegates from cloud+median; the sort exposes their rate NULL so they sort last + display `—`, disclosed). The carve-out is **population-correctness, not outlier-trimming** — the real high-miss tail is a *voting* member (Wilson 29%), not the delegates (oddities). See roadmap "Also (HO 527)" / "Also (HO 535)" + DONE tombstones. **Still banked from the arc:** a **career missed-rate sort** (NULL-coverage set differs — the dotplot-career-toggle reason) and a **career chamber-median context** on the hub (deliberately unbuilt to avoid a 4-stat hero). *Gate:* Corey's appetite for the career cuts. *Owner:* Corey.
- **Prod arm for the `unstable_cache` key-rotation clause (LOW — the mechanism is already grounded in source).** *Owner:* Code. The oddity *An `unstable_cache` key hashes the callback's own source text* (HO 647) states a prod consequence — a rotated key has no prod entry, so the first post-deploy read is a cold miss that builds the new value directly — which is **derived, not observed**. The derivation is sound and handler-agnostic (`unstable-cache.ts:133-134`: `generateCacheKey` receives `fixedKey` + args in Next's shared path *before* any cache handler is consulted), which is why a local `.next/cache` reading carries to the Data Cache at all. It stayed unobserved because **the band's public surface cannot discriminate** — a fat payload is a superset of a thin one and `AbsenceCardBack` renders byte-identically from either — so no number of prod hits settles it. **Design: TWO arms, with OPPOSITE predicted readings, because one arm alone confirms whichever way it lands.** *Arm A* — a deploy touching nothing inside `queryAbsenceWatch`'s body — predicts key **stable** → cache HIT → log **ABSENT** (clause (b), callees are not in the key). *Arm B* — a deploy that edits the body — predicts key **rotates** → MISS → log **PRESENT** (the clause under test). **Arm A NEEDS A POSITIVE CONTROL, because its pass condition is the ABSENCE of a signal** — and an absent log is indistinguishable from a log path that never worked. `/members` calls `getMemberCardExpansion` **directly**, so hit `/members` in the same deploy and confirm the log fires there: the same shared call site that makes the log noisy is what proves it is wired. Without that control arm A is unreadable, and this is the piece the first draft of the design was missing. **Three traps.** (1) **The callback-side instrument is invalid:** a log added *inside* the callback changes its text, rotates the key, guarantees a miss, and so manufactures its own pass — the same-as-success shape entered through the instrument rather than the metric. (2) The log must therefore sit in a **callee**, whose text is not in the key — but `getMemberCardExpansion` is **also called by `/members`**, so it must **identify its caller**. Note this supersedes the tempting alternative of hiding it in a callee only the band reaches: that variant destroys the positive control above, so caller-tagging is required rather than optional. (3) **Read the two outcomes asymmetrically — ABSENT is unambiguous, PRESENT is not.** ABSENT means key stable → hit → the callback never ran (given the control passed). PRESENT has three live explanations: the body genuinely rotated the key (arm B), the bundler **inlined** the callee — which folds the log's own string literal into `cb.toString()`, rotating the key and making the log **fire**, so inlining can never produce a null reading — or the entry was simply gone for an unrelated reason (TTL expiry, a `revalidateTag("votes")` from the votes cron, eviction, a cold region). So arm B confirms nothing on its own; it is arm A's absence, backed by the control, that carries the result. *Gate:* a session already deploying for another reason can take arm A for free; do not spend a deploy on this. *Close:* arm A reads ABSENT with its control firing, or the prod clause is corrected in the oddity. **Deliberately LOW: the cost of the prod arm exceeds what proving it buys, and the clause is flagged derived-and-unfalsified where it lives.**

## WATCH

- **WATCH (HO 731) · a top-N primary's marked set is NOT the top N by vote share, by design — and no instrument may assert that it is.** opened 2026-09-17; *Owner:* Code, standing. Read on the AK at-large page from the bytes the parser itself read: the four rows carrying `class="results_row  winner"` are Begich 44.6% · Bill Hill 32.5% · Hafner 3.8% · Williams 2.7%, while **Matthew Schultz at 8.1% — third by share — is unmarked, and his row carries `(Unofficially withdrew)`**. Alaska replaces a withdrawn top-four advancer with the next finisher, so the marked set is `{1, 2, 4, 5}` and the page, the parser and our stored rows agree cell for cell. The same day `senate-AK-2026-open`'s marked set **is** its top four, so the shape is contest-specific and a spot check of one contest proves nothing about another. **Why this is a line and not a fix:** there is no defect anywhere — the scheduled recovery worked, and `isWinner` is a faithful per-row class test. It is here because the next reader who finds `{1, 2, 4, 5}` will otherwise diagnose a parse bug that is not there. **The invariant never to write:** *the `winner` rows are the top N by `vote_pct`*. A check built on it would be the count-invariant-blind-to-its-own-source shape (method § Gates) — both sides descending from the same scrape, agreeing with each other while describing the wrong rule. *Close:* none by criterion; it leaves by tombstone if Ballotpedia ever stops marking withdrawn-replacement advancers. *Reads if never touched:* four `winner` rows whose shares are not the four highest, on a page that is correct.


- **Three `/` schedule readings are OWED because the shown day has one row, and the column desync among them is the one a reader could mistake for a defect (opened HO 734, 2026-09-18).** opened 2026-09-18; *Owner:* Code, low. HO 734 put `HearingPanel` in flow under a `.hsch-row` and ruled that an open panel **grows its own column** while the other column does not move — the panel's box measured **685px WIDE** at 2560 against its row's own **685px width**, the panel staying inside its column rather than spanning the wrap — so at `>=1700px` with `rows.length >= 6` the two columns will visibly differ in height while a row is open. **That is the accepted shape, not a defect:** the panel belongs to its row, C7 governs the box, and lifting it out to span both columns would restructure the grid HO 682 measured one level at a time. It is filed because the first person to see it at 2560 will reach for a bug report. **Why it is owed and not read:** the day HO 734 shipped on carried **one** row, and the week ribbon is a readout rather than a control (`.hsch-cell` has no `onClick`), so the shown day cannot be navigated to a fuller one. Three readings are unreachable on a one-row day and **none of them was recorded as a pass**: (1) a second `/` row replacing the first, (2) single-open **across** the two columns, (3) the `.hsch-now` marker staying above its row with that row open (`markerAt` needs `firstUpcoming > 0`, i.e. at least two rows). The multi-row half of the shape WAS read on `/hearings`, which runs the same `openId` state on 61 entries — a second entry replaces the first, one panel at a time — so what is unread is this surface's wiring of a shape already proven, not the shape. *Close:* on the next day the schedule renders **>= 6** rows, take `card-overlap-730.ts` at 2560 with a column-A row opened, plus one capture pair (column A open, column B open), and record the height difference as the shape; the three readings above go with it. *Reads if never done:* a ruling made on one row, and a 2560 render nobody has looked at with both columns populated.

- **`.hearing-panel`'s base `row` variant sits 2px right of the column it aligns to, at every width (found HO 734, 2026-09-18).** opened 2026-09-18; *Owner:* Code, low. Found by HO 734's indent gate while proving the ladder property, and **left alone deliberately**. `.hearing-row-btn`'s tracks are PX (`grid-template-columns: 14px 50px 90px minmax(0, 1fr) auto`, `padding: 8px 12px`, `gap: 10px`), so unlike `--agenda` and `--sched` this variant does **not** move with the `--fs-*` ladder: on `/committee/hlig00` its `padding-left: 38px` reads caption `55.00` against `.hearing-time` `53.00` — **drift 2.00px at 1440 AND 2.00px at 2560, LADDER 0.00px**. The sum of its declared tracks is `12px + 14px + 10px = 36px`, so the 38 is 2px past the track it was *"tuned to"* (`app/globals.css:9343`). **It is a constant offset and not HO 734's class**, which is why the gate reports it and does not gate on it: changing it would move a shipped surface on `/committee/[systemCode]` and `/bill/[id]` for a reason the HO 734 ruling did not cover. *Close:* either 38 becomes the declared sum (`12px + 14px + 10px`) with a capture pair on both surfaces showing the 2px close, **or** the 2px is recorded as deliberate — a comment naming what it is for, since *"tuned to"* does not say whether the 2 is intentional. *Reads if never done:* a panel whose two other variants are exact sums and whose base is a number 2px off one.


- **New (HO 684) · the NEWS chip's 2-line clamp bites at 2560, and the reason is the type ladder rather than the width.** *Owner:* Code. The clamp was ruled over a single-line ellipsis on the criterion that at **the tightest box** — the 400px `auto-fit` floor, 430 viewport — the live-max **95-character** headline resolves in full across two lines, with the clamp only firing past ~120 ch against a corpus max of 123. **That criterion holds and was verified.** What it could not anticipate is that the tightest box in *pixels* is not the tightest in *characters*: at **2560** the C2 type ladder lifts `--fs-10` to 13px while `auto-fit` simultaneously narrows the card to ~439px, so one of the six live headlines clamps there (honestly, with a visible ellipsis) while every headline resolves at 430, 1440 and 1920. *Measured 2026-09-02, six featured seats, headline lengths 51/57/58/78/79/95.* **Not a defect and deliberately not fixed** — truncating the longest headline at one width is what a clamp is for, and widening it unilaterally would have overridden a ruling. *Watch for:* the clamp biting **more than one** headline at 2560, or biting at 1920 — either means the corpus has lengthened past what the 2-line budget holds, at which point the options are a 3rd line at ≥2400 or scoping the NEWS body's font off the ladder. *Instrument:* `scripts/diagnostic/races-chips-captures-684.ts` (local, prod build) prints chars, line count and `clamped` per chip per viewport. **The figures above are a reading of one day's headlines, not a property of the design.**


- **New (HO 645) · the amber tier is UNEXERCISED on the live corpus.** *Owner:* Code. `[8, 30)` is **empty** today (M3: MIA 2, AT RISK 0, pooled 2 — both live members are past 30), which HO 642's occupancy replay predicted: the House's most recent roll in that window is weeks old. It was fired against real data before shipping per §2c — `ABSENCE_STREAK_MIN` temporarily 40, which moved Wilson (streak 34) through the amber path: header rendered `▲ MIA (1) · AT RISK (1)`, the card took the amber frame + amber-bright disc + `--text-secondary` surname, and the back opened with its amber frame; reverted, nothing committed. **That is a falsification, not coverage**: the branch has still never run on its own predicate in production, so the first genuine amber member is worth eyeballing rather than assuming. *Close:* a real member lands in `[8, 30)` and the band is looked at.
- **New (HO 646) · the `card === null` CAUSE is unfired.** *Owner:* Code. HO 645 proved the **render path** by forcing the null at the band's assembly site — complete back, three ruled rows of base-query data, **zero** block elements rather than empty shells, live affordance, against a control member reading 2 blocks / 0 empty-shelled in the same page load. **That forced null sits DOWNSTREAM of `queryAbsenceWatch`'s per-member `try/catch`**, so the catch itself has still never run: neither its `console.error("[absence-watch] card assembly failed for …")` nor the `Promise.all` isolation that is supposed to keep one member's assembly failure off the others. The render path and the failure path are separate claims and only one of them has been exercised — recorded so a later reader does not read HO 645's PASS as covering both. *Promote when:* a real assembly failure appears in logs, or the catch is opened for any other reason.
- **New (HO 646) · `SponsorPhoto`'s fallback branch on `/members` is proven by inspection, not live (accepted residue).** *Owner:* Code. HO 645 gave `SponsorPhoto` an explicit-height prop and preserved the served class string character for character, which is what stands in for §5.7's un-run byte diff. **That proof covers the PHOTO path only** — `/members?expanded=` renders **0** instances of the initials branch (every member on that page resolves a bioguide photo), so the fallback's markup was read rather than measured. Not a defect and not worth manufacturing a 404 to exercise; filed so the gap is on the record rather than inside a claim of byte-identity. *Close:* a member with no resolvable photo renders on `/members` and the branch is looked at, or the claim is narrowed in place to name the photo path only.

*Standing observations; each notes what would promote it.*

- **The doc-sweep deletion gate is `git diff --numstat`'s deletions column — `grep -c '^-[^-]'` is not a deletion count (owed from HO 639–641, filed HO 643).** *Owner:* Code/Corey, standing. In unified diff a deleted markdown bullet `- **text**` renders as `-- **text**`, which `^-[^-]` **excludes by construction** — so the count reads **0** on a diff that deleted bullets, and 0 is indistinguishable from a clean append. It was reported as verification by two different readers across the 639–641 arc before anything caught it. Use **`git diff --numstat HEAD~1 HEAD -- <file>`** and read the deletions column (normal-format `^<` also works). **Do NOT diff against `git show HEAD:file`** — `core.autocrlf` is true with no `.gitattributes`, so an LF blob against a CRLF worktree reports every line changed. Mechanism is filed in oddities as instance 3 of the proxy-instrument family. *Promote to SKILL as a doc-sweep verification rule when:* a third arc either misreports on it or has to rediscover it — two arcs is an oddity, three is a convention.

- **A DOCUMENTED COVERAGE RATIO GOES FALSE WHEN ITS DENOMINATOR GROWS, AND NOTHING RE-RUNS THE BACKFILL THAT WOULD FIX IT (opened HO 643).** *Owner:* Code/Corey, standing. Two halves, and the first is why the second stays invisible.
  **(a) The doc-form failure.** A sentence of the form *"N of M, gaps are X and Y"* is **exhaustive when written and stays grammatically true forever**. When M grows, the sentence does not become ungrammatical, self-contradictory, or greppable — **no word in it changes**, so no sweep, no typecheck and no diff flags it. It just quietly stops meaning what it says: the gap list goes from *complete* to *a sample*, and a reader with no reason to doubt it treats the un-listed remainder as non-existent. This is the measured-number rule's blind spot — that rule catches a figure that is *wrong*, and this failure keeps the figure *right* while the ratio around it rots.
  **(b) The operational gap.** **There is no trigger that re-runs a backfill when its parent set is re-seeded.** The backfills are manual/quarterly and the sets they cover (`race_ratings` re-seeds, `sync:members`) grow independently, so every re-seed silently widens every ratio taken over it. **Un-run is not un-resolvable**, and the docs cannot tell them apart: `backfill:2024-margin` has reached 106 House rated seats against a House rated set of **151**, so ~**45** have no margin because nobody has re-run it — while the entry's two named gaps (ME-02 RCV, TX-09 uncontested) describe seats that are genuinely unresolvable. Same shape one table over: `sync:crosswalk` is gated on `members`, so a member added by a later `sync:members` sits `skipped_no_member` until it is re-run by hand (the Gallagher CA-01 instance is already in SKILL).
  **Exhibits.** **(1) the challenger harvest — see the harvest-currency QUEUED line above**, which owns the scoping and is not duplicated here; it is the fullest instance (reach frozen at `primary_date` 2026-06-02, 57/186). **(2) `backfill:2024-margin`, 106/151** — corrected in SKILL at HO 643 with its gap list explicitly de-exhausted, NOT re-run. **(3) the rated-set predicate figures themselves** — 137/44 and 61 drifted to 186/46 and 59 with nothing re-checking them, which is how the class was noticed at all. **Also in the class, dated at HO 643 and therefore accurate TODAY rather than fixed:** incumbent cash 177/186, Kalshi 185/186, incumbent photo 184/186 — one denominator, and they will all go stale together at the next re-seed. **Explicit NON-member, because it shows what the class is actually about:** `backfill:primary-results` states ~47% and is *not* in it — the forward sync fills each contest as it votes, so it **names its own trigger** and self-heals. The defect is the absent trigger, not the ratio.
  *Promote to SKILL as an authoring convention when:* a **second arc** hits this — three exhibits inside ONE arc is an oddity with three instances, not yet a convention, and promoting on a single arc's evidence would be the same over-reach the two-arcs rule exists to prevent. **That rule is being applied to itself here, deliberately.** The convention it would become: *a documented ratio over a growing set carries its denominator's provenance and its backfill's last-run reach, or it states no gap list at all.*

- **`primaries.primary_type` is NULL on 91 of 876 rows and the MECHANISM IS UNKNOWN (HO 641, measured HO 640).** *Owner:* Code. **91 NULL of 876 overall; 75 NULL of the 718 completed**, spread across 12 state+chamber groups — NY house 36, AZ house 14, CT house 10, **WA house 10**, NV 5, UT 5, HI 4, LA senate 2, VT 2, GA senate 1, PA 1, SC 1. **WA is the only special-system state left NULL**: CA `top_two` 52, AK `top_four` 4, ME `ranked_choice` 6, LA `jungle` 6 / `open` 2 / NULL 2 — and WA **NULL 10**. **Mechanism unknown, in those words.** `primaryTypeFor` (`lib/primary-calendar-scrape.ts:62-67`) is **ruled out**: its fallback is `return "open"`, so it cannot emit NULL. Nothing else has been read. **This is NOT the HO 584 fallback-default WATCH** — that one is about `open` being an unverified catch-all across ~45 states, a different value and a different mechanism, and citing it here would file a live gap under a cause that reads as already understood. **Consequence, and it is the reason this is worth carrying:** a branch on `primary_type = 'top_two'` catches **52 CA and 0 WA** — it silently misses an entire top-two state, and the miss looks like an absence of data rather than a bug. *Promote* the moment anything is about to branch on this column; read the write path before writing the branch. **HO 750 (2026-09-27): a branch arrived and missed Washington exactly as this line says.** HO 750's first build of the ballot-sourced roster read top-two and top-four from `primary_type`, and its review caught Washington's 11 non-incumbent rows published at `won_primary`. The harvest now takes the systems from the calendar's own sets (`TOP_TWO_STATES`, `TOP_FOUR_STATES` in `lib/primary-calendar-scrape.ts`, exported for it), not the column. The primary-sourced fallback's `CASE` (HO 736) still branches on the column and would label a Washington race `won_primary` if it lost its box; none has. Not promoted: nothing new branches on the column. The mechanism is still unknown.

- **The candidate status-precedence ladder is transcribed TWICE in `lib/queries.ts` with no shared constant (HO 638).** *Owner:* Code. `getRaceCandidates` (the `/race/[id]` hub + the prefetched dashboard card) and `getRaceCandidatesForCycle` (`/electoral`) each carry their own `CASE … WHEN status IN ('won_primary','nominee') THEN 0 …` rung list. HO 638 updated both and left cross-referencing comments at each site, so they are **in step today**. **What makes this a WATCH rather than a nit is the failure mode: a status added to one and missed in the other does not error — it falls to the `ELSE` rung and sorts the new status BELOW withdrawn candidates**, i.e. a silently wrong order on exactly the surface the new status was added to serve. *Promote* when a third status enters the vocabulary, or when either ladder is opened for any other reason — extract the rung mapping to one constant then. Same shape as the four inline `median()` copies (HO 427/428): duplicated-and-currently-correct, with no mechanism that keeps it correct.

- **`seed:races` validates ratings but NOT candidate status — an invalid value inserts silently and sorts to `ELSE` (HO 638).** *Owner:* Code. `scripts/seed-races.ts` carries a `VALID_RATINGS` set and warns-and-skips an unrecognized rating (deliberately non-fatal, so one bad rating can't abort a seed run); the candidate `status` field goes straight into the upsert as `c.status ?? null` with **no membership check at all**. A typo — `nominnee`, `Nominee` — therefore **inserts clean**, and because both ordering ladders route anything unrecognized to the `ELSE` rung, it renders in the **wrong position rather than erroring**. **This is why HO 638 read the raw status column back as the literal string `nominee` before trusting either ordering fire: at the seed step, a typo and a correct value produce a byte-identical failure signature.** *Promote* on the second status typo reaching the table, or fold a `VALID_STATUSES` set in beside `VALID_RATINGS` the next time `seed-races.ts` is opened for any reason — the surrounding pattern already exists, so it is a ride-along, not a build.


- **`memberVotesHealPending` pinned (HO 567).** *Owner:* Code. A transient publication race shows an id once, then healed the next run; a **permanently memberless upstream roll** (the STEP-0-failure case arriving later) shows the same id **PINNED** across runs, retried forever per the 553 posture. *Promote* to a bound (the stamp-after-N debate, weighed against re-opening a permanent drop) only if pinned on more than a handful. *Instrument:* `cron_runs.payload` `memberVotesHealPending` run-over-run, both chambers.
- **`amendment_votes` vocabulary rot (HO 566 M4).** *Owner:* Code. INERT today (97/97, drift 0/0, unmatched 0). *Promote* to a vocabulary fix if M4 ever reads nonzero drift or unmatched — a new Senate question format outside `SENATE_AMDT_QUESTION_LIKE` thinning new links silently. *Instrument:* `scripts/diagnostic/delete-rebuild-erosion-566.ts` M4; re-run on any amendment-vote / vocabulary touch, or whenever the probe runs for anything else.
- **`rosterDeletesRefused` pinned (HO 568).** *Owner:* Code. A one-tick refusal is the guard doing its job; the **same code refused across consecutive ticks** means the source is serving empty for a rostered committee — investigate the source, **never loosen the gate**. Composes with the probe's M1(c) staleness read (a refused committee's `updated_at` freezes). *Instrument:* the committees `cron_runs.payload` `rosterDeletesRefused` run-over-run.
- **The 32 non-parsing House districts (HO 564 repair residual).** *Owner:* Code. Sixteen districts (AL-02 · AR-01/03 · ME-01/02 · NC-02 · NY-02/05/08/16/18/20/22/26 · UT-04) return `no_section` after three retries, so their rosters stay empty **by design** — the HO 564 guard's whole purpose is refusing to invent data, and re-scraping cannot recover a page that doesn't parse. **Do NOT "fix" by loosening the guard.** *Promote/close:* periodic re-check — the HO 563 probe re-runs cheaply against exactly these sixteen; if a page starts parsing, the next cursor visit repopulates it with no intervention. *Instrument:* `scripts/diagnostic/zero-candidate-primaries-563.ts` (M2 `no_section` bucket) against the sixteen.
- **`no_candidates` is trusted as authoritative for row creation (HO 564).** *Owner:* Code. A parsing page with zero candidates creates a `primaries` row (a legitimately uncontested contest is a real empty row); if Ballotpedia ever serves a **partially-rendered** page that parses but yields nothing, that becomes a row asserting an empty contest. **Inert today** — 17 such rows, all cross-checked against a rostered sibling. *Promote* if an empty-contest row ever appears where the **sibling is also empty** AND the state's other districts are rostered (the signature of a bad parse, not a real uncontested race). *Instrument:* the HO 563 probe's `no_candidates` bucket vs sibling-roster presence.
- **The SC Aug 11 special-primary WATCH (HO 561 -> 600 -> 601).** *Owner:* Code. **Three HO 600 premises corrected on measurement, recorded here because this entry repeated them:** `/race/S-SC-2026` was **never in scope** (the race hub reads `getRunoffsForRace`, which filters `election_round='runoff'` and returns **0 rows**); the phantom D row's footprint was a **count inflation** (the Aug 11 tick read 48 contests, now 47) and **not a rendered label** anywhere; and `getDashboardPrimaries` runs unconditionally in `CompetitiveRacesBlock` regardless of variant, so the PRIMARIES tab renders on **both** `/` and `/dashboard-classic`. The ingestion hit branch is now **EXERCISED** — HO 601 wrote the 10-name Aug 11 field to `senate-SC-2026-special-R` from a real run. The old close criterion (*"results present in `senate-SC-2026-special-{D,R}` by roughly Aug 13"*) is **RETIRED as unmeetable**: there is no Democratic contest on Aug 11 (S.C. Code §7-11-55, single-party by construction when the deceased nominee had general-election opposition), so the `{D,R}` form read FAIL while the system was correct, and HO 600 deleted the phantom D row. Replacement, adopted verbatim from HO 600 §4 — **three criteria, staged because roster and results are different failures**: **C1 (roster, by 2026-08-10)** `senate-SC-2026-special-R` carries **10** `primary_candidates` rows matching the published field — *now PASSING* (was the point of the criterion: it read FAIL on the day it was written); **C2 (results, by 2026-08-13)** ≥1 of those rows has `vote_pct NOT NULL`, with `status='winner'` on 1 (outright) or 2 (runoff advancers, the HO 207 two-★ rule); **C3 (standing regression guard)** `senate-SC-2026-special-D` does not exist. *Instrument:* the cron payload's `priorityScraped` object — now `{attemptedIds, populatedIds, fetchFailures, settledSkipped, emptyRosterSkipped, sources}` after HO 601 C3, so it **can** read FAIL (the pre-601 `string[]` could not) — plus `scripts/diagnostic/special-router-falsify-601.ts`.

- **The HO 560 settled-row freeze is STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE for substitution (HO 601).** *Owner:* Code. The freeze was built for one hazard: Ballotpedia rebuilding a seat's page around a LATER contest, so a delete-rebuild overwrites a past election's results with a different contest's field. **The HO 601 router removes that hazard upstream** — a special-classified box never routes to the base id — so the freeze can no longer *observe* a substituting roster. Leg 2's `isSettled consulted and REFUSED: true` is real and was reported honestly, but **the payload it refused was the correct June roster, not a substituting one** (and `isSettled` is evaluated before the roster is even built, so it short-circuits unconditionally). The guard **stays live and useful for its general case** — any rewrite of a settled row: Ballotpedia editing a past result, a re-scrape parsing differently — and leg 3's OH `settledSkipped` is exactly that case firing. Its status is **second line behind the router**, not first line against substitution. Do NOT record it as "exercised for substitution"; that reading is available from every individually-true line of the HO 601 ship report and is the thing this entry exists to block. *Promote:* if the router is ever narrowed or bypassed — which re-exposes the freeze and makes its substitution behavior testable again. *Instrument:* `scripts/diagnostic/special-router-falsify-601.ts` leg 2 (it prints which branch fired, incl. the `neither (never reached)` case).

- **The 15 gap-state house primary dates are frozen (HO 577).** *Owner:* Code. `calByState` now reads only the clean regular statewide senate row, so the 15 states with no 2026 Senate seat — **AZ CA CT HI IN MD MO ND NV NY PA UT VT WA WI** (HO 209's 35-state gate) — have **no clean source**; the House write COALESCEs, preserving each row's existing date and **never refreshing it**. Strictly better than the old circular self-reference, but a real latent staleness: if a gap state's actual primary date moves, the stored date won't follow, and nobody watching the code would see it. *Promote to a fix* — give those states a statewide source (the HO 209 coverage question, its own item) — if any gap state's real primary date moves. *Instrument:* spot-check two of the fifteen against the public primary calendar. **INSTRUMENT RUN 2026-09-03 AND PASSED, 2 of 15 (HO 688, discharging an observation held from the HO 686 close).** **AZ** — frozen `2026-07-21` is **correct at source**: the live Ballotpedia AZ-03 page carries "July 21, 2026", and all **18** AZ House rows agree on that date. **WA** — frozen `2026-08-04` matches its real top-two date. So **no live 2026 date remains for a real-world move to diverge from**, and the residual latent staleness targets the **2028-cycle refresh** rather than anything this cycle — which is what makes leaving this open cheap rather than merely cautious. Re-run the bounding read as `SELECT state, MIN(primary_date), MAX(primary_date), COUNT(*) FROM primaries WHERE election_round='primary' AND district IS NOT NULL AND state IN (<the fifteen>) GROUP BY state`. *Promote unchanged:* if any gap state's real primary date moves.

- **`amendment_votes` walk `deferred` counter — transient-vs-pinned instrument (HO 552/553).** *Owner:* Code. `walkAmendmentVotes` now returns `deferred` = HAMDTs left unstamped because a constructed `voteId` had no `votes` row yet (pending, not settled — retried next run, the HO 552 non-stamp fix). **HO 553 STEP 0 proved the branch fires** (scratch-perturbed `voteId` → `deferred=1`, unstamped, retry completed on restore — recorded as **proven protection, not an untriggered number**). The reading to watch: a **transient** sync lag shows `deferred` non-zero on one run and back to 0 the next; a **permanent** gap (a `voteId` that never syncs) shows `deferred` **PINNED** across consecutive runs with `remaining` stable — the walk re-fetches `/actions` (one GET) per stuck HAMDT every run indefinitely. The fix adopted is the **ownership split**: decide which file OWNS a fact so the other points at it instead of restating it. **What's different about the fourth — why this is a promotion, not another failure:** HO 505 wrote the trigger down *in advance* ("if you catch yourself writing the same sentence into both files, that's the fourth instance"), and it **fired during this sweep, pre-commit** — SKILL's first draft restated the oddities guard-inversion mechanism, was caught against that trigger, and cut to a pointer before the commit landed (oddities owns the mechanism + the 73-test numbers; SKILL names it and links). So the ownership split now works as a **checkable pre-commit rule**, not just a post-hoc catch — the practice succeeded on contact. **Open question (the real residue):** nothing *enforces* the split when no handoff is scripting the check — it held here only because HO 505 had the rule in hand. *Close / next:* decide whether the split can be made self-enforcing (e.g. a doc-test that flags a distinctive sentence appearing in both files) or whether it stays a discipline that depends on someone holding the handoff — until then it's a promoted-but-unenforced practice, not a solved problem. **Fifth instance (HO 538):** SKILL's `getBillAmendmentVotes` line restated the stale "~114" Senate count that HO 537 M8 had corrected to 97 — caught only by *reading the SKILL diff out of the `537-review` ref* (the review-ref route, now written into SKILL.md), not by anything enforcing the split; still unenforced.

- **The backlog-claim convention problem — promote from a header note to a decision (HO 570).** *Owner:* Corey/Code. The header rule ("a backlog line's mechanism is a claim; probe before building") has now been tripped enough that "check before building" is doing the work a convention should do: two wrong-mechanism claims in three HOs (HO 512/514), and HO 570 alone added **two more** (the `.v2f-sponsor*` dead-set glob; the "HO 502 redirect pattern" that doesn't exist) plus stale line numbers in three places. It's past the threshold. **The decision owed** is what changes: whether a stale/wrong line gets **corrected in place when a probe disproves it** (the HO 512/514/546 precedent, done ad hoc), whether entries carry a **verified-at** stamp so age is visible, or whether it stays a discipline that depends on someone holding a handoff. Same shape as the **doc-duplication WATCH above** — promoted-but-unenforced since HO 505. Frame it as a decision, not a chore.
- **Guard-rejection telemetry (HO 239).** `[stage] rejected impossible downgrade` / `[stage] downgrade pending` warns are **flicker-rate telemetry**: rejection frequency is the live signal of classifier noise, and it **informs the action-code tier's priority** (BANKED). *Promote* the action-code tier when rejection frequency climbs or when a stage-gated surface ships. *Instrument:* grep the summarize cron's 30-min log window for `[stage]`.

- **B-sync transition-logging path unproven (HO 383).** 383's **sync-side** stage-transition logging is typechecked but only fires when a live sync observes a real stage advance (vs the summarize runner, which logs on re-summarize). *Promote / close:* on the first natural stage advance after `cae39fa`, confirm a `stage_transitions` row written **by sync** (not the runner), that it surfaces on `/changes`, and that the runner **noops** on the later re-summarize (no duplicate row). *Instrument:* `validate-stage-transitions-232.ts` + eyeball `/changes` after the first observed advance.
- **Manual `sync:votes` leaves `member_participation` stale (HO 595/596).** *Owner:* Code. The participation refresh hangs off the **route** `/api/sync-votes`, not off the writers. `npm run sync:votes` / `npm run sync:senate-votes` call `runVotesSync` / `runSenateVotesSync` directly, so a manual run writes `member_votes` **without** triggering the refresh and the materialized table sits stale until the next `0 10 * * *` cron. *Instrument (this is the whole reason it exists):* the `refreshed_at` stamp — compare it against `MAX(vote_date)`; `scripts/diagnostic/participation-falsify-595.ts --stamp` prints it. There is **no standalone refresh entry point** today. *Close:* either hang `refreshMemberParticipation()` off the **writer** (`lib/votes-sync.ts` / `lib/senate-votes-sync.ts`) so every write path triggers it, or add an `npm run refresh:participation`. The writer option is the better shape — it makes the invariant "member_votes changed implies the table was rebuilt" structural rather than conventional.
- **Participation refresh headroom (HO 595).** *Owner:* Code. The refresh's own worst was **11.95s WITH the covering index** (M3), which is why it runs on a **dedicated 30s client** rather than `getDb()`'s 10s — the refresh breaches the very bound it exists to let the request path escape. Without the index it is 15.44s median / 27.14s worst, so the index is now load-bearing on the **refresh** path (its purpose inverted; see SKILL). *Watch:* that 30s headroom shrinks as `member_votes` grows (365,996 rows today, ~661/member). *Promote* if the refresh ever times out — the `[participation] refresh-failed:` log line and a non-advancing `refreshed_at` are the tells, and the non-destructive path means a failure shows up as **stale, not empty**. Fix direction would be an incremental refresh (only members whose votes changed) rather than a full rebuild.
